- Branch: `claude/p2-29`
- Phase: Phase 2: Open polls end to end at Tier 0

## Scope

The gate before any official work: every everyday surface goes through at least three design iterations for UX quality ([[PROJECT_BRIEF]], Priority).

Surfaces: home, the stream and its card, topic and place pages, search, the poll page (open, live, sealed, closed), answering in the ballot client (arrive, questions, check, sending, done, receipt, change your answer, recovery), creating a poll, the author's list, sharing surfaces, the account and profile pages, notifications, and the system pages.

Each iteration is one PR per surface (or a small group):

1. **Review**: screenshots at 360px and desktop width, light and dark, of every state; a written critique against [[language]], the brief's design direction and the Center for Civic Design rules for the ballot; tap counts and copy read aloud; what a first-time user would misread.
2. **Fix**: the changes the review calls for, with the stories and tests updated.
3. **Record**: the before and after screenshots and what changed, in this file.

A surface is done after its third iteration finds nothing it would change. The everyday app is complete when every surface is done and [[P2-23]] to [[P2-28]] are done; then official work may start.

## Iterations

Screenshots are taken from Storybook (`vp exec storybook dev` in the app, then Playwright at 360px and 1280px, light and dark) and are not committed: the stories are the reproducible record, and the repository holds no binary images. Each record below says what the screenshots showed.

### System pages: iteration 1

Not found, Something went wrong and You're offline (`apps/web/src/components/site-layout.tsx`; stories `Components/Site layout`).

Review:

- **The offline page's "Try again" drew at 12px** where the error page's drew at 16px. A link styled with `buttonVariants()` keeps both the base's `text-xs` and the size's `text-base`, and unmerged, the stylesheet's order picks `text-xs`. Every link or plain button styled that way had it: the home page's "Browse polls", the account's new-poll link, the stream's start link, the poll register's filter button and the verification page's action. Only `Button` merged its classes.
- **The offline page offered other places to go** that all fail offline the same way, so each one would bring back this page.
- **Not found spoke the civic register**: "board entries, definitions and results are permanent" and "paste its 64-character ID into the form on the front page". Someone who followed a broken link to a friend's poll has a link, not an ID, and none of those words.
- **Other places to go left out Discover**, the everyday way to find a poll again, and its three links left an empty cell in the two-column grid at desktop width.
- Kept: the titles say what happened in plain words; the error page says nothing about the error (T-67) and tells a voter their ballot is safe in the ballot app; both themes pass axe; one action per page, in `primary`.
- For a later iteration (the frame, not these pages): at 360px the site's links wrap to a second row with "Moderation log" alone on it.

Fix:

- `packages/ui`'s `buttonVariants` returns merged classes (`cn`), so a link drawn as a button takes its size's text; the `AsLink` button story checks the computed size.
- The offline page has no "Other places to go"; not found and error list Discover ("Search the questions people are asking"), Polls, Moderation log and Home, four for the grid.
- Not found: "The link may be mistyped or cut short. Polls and their results are never deleted, so a poll's own link keeps working." and "Looking for a poll someone shared? Ask them to send the link again, or search for its question on Discover."
- The site-layout stories check the four places, no civic wording on not found, and on the offline page no places and a 16px "Try again".

Record: before, the offline page's button label was visibly smaller than its body copy, and the desktop grid ended in a blank cell; after, the button matches the error page's, the grid closes at four, and the offline page ends at its button.

### System pages: iteration 2

The same three pages and stories, after iteration 1.

Review:

- **The offline page still offered other places to go, in the frame.** Iteration 1 took the list out of the page's body, but the header's four site links, Account and the wordmark's link home, and the footer's four links, were all still there, and offline every one of them brings back this same page.
- **The error page put three things in one paragraph**: what happened, what to do, and that a ballot in progress is safe. The last is the one a voter came for, and it sat at the end of a five-line block at 360px. Not found and offline already split the plain account from the secondary note.
- **"Error" and "Offline" above the titles only repeated them** ("OFFLINE", then "You're offline"), a monospace label the everyday register keeps for evidence ([[language]]). Not found's "404" is different: it's the one code people know and quote when they ask for help.
- **"Every poll this server lists"**: a visitor who followed a broken link knows a site, not a server.
- **"Every moderation decision, with its reason"** didn't say why a lost visitor would go there. The likely reason is a poll they can't find in search because moderation left it out.
- Kept: the 404's four places and their order, one primary action per page, the 16px "Try again", both themes passing axe.
- Still for the frame (not these pages): at 360px "Moderation log" wraps alone onto the header's second row, and the footer's top margin leaves a tall empty band under a short page.

Fix:

- `SiteLayout` takes `links={false}`, which draws the wordmark as plain text and leaves out the site's links in the header and the footer; the offline page (`src/app/offline-page.tsx`) uses it, so its only links are "Skip to content" and "Try again". The header keeps its height.
- The error page's ballot sentence is its own smaller paragraph under the account of what happened.
- The system page's code label is optional, and only not found has one.
- "Every poll listed on this site"; "Why a poll was hidden or left out of search".
- Tests: the offline stories check there's no navigation and exactly those two links; the served `/offline` document (`tests/ssr.test.ts`) links only to `#main` and its own address; the error story checks the ballot paragraph is separate and no label precedes the title; not found's places say nothing about a server.

Record: before, the offline page at 360px opened with five links in the header and closed with four in the footer, each a dead end; after, it shows the wordmark, the notice, "Try again" and the footer's claim. The error page's ballot reassurance now stands as its own paragraph above the button, and neither the error nor the offline title has a label repeating it.

### System pages: iteration 3

The same three pages and stories, after iteration 2, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844 and 1280 wide, light and dark.

Review:

- **On a small phone the page's action was below the first screen.** At 360 by 640 the error page's "Try again" started at 608px and ended at 664px; at 320 by 568 it started at 632px. Not found's first place to go, Discover, started at 717px at both sizes, so the first screen held a header, a title and copy, and nothing to tap but the frame's links. The 48px title took three or four lines, and the header's links take 177px (below).
- **Not found's second paragraph repeated the row under it**: "search for its question on Discover", right above a Discover row saying "Search the questions people are asking".
- **Still for the frame (not these pages), measured**: at every phone width the header's links wrap onto a third row ("Discover, Polls, How it works" and then "Moderation log" alone), making the header 177px tall, more than a quarter of a 640px screen. The four links are 307px wide at 16px with no gaps, more than the 288px a 320px screen leaves, so no gap or margin change fits them on one row. It needs a design decision for the frame: fewer links on phones, or a menu.
- Kept: the desktop layout (unchanged at 1280), the 16px "Try again", one primary action per page, the offline page's two links, the error page's separate ballot paragraph, and both themes passing axe.

Fix:

- Below 640px the title is 36px (`text-4xl`), then 60px up to 1024px (72px from 640px before) and 72px from there; the gaps above and between the title, copy and places tighten on phones only.
- Not found: "Looking for a poll someone shared? Ask them to send the link again, or search for it below."
- Stories `ErrorPhone` and `NotFoundPhone` run at the preview's 360px phone viewport (in both Storybook test projects) and check that "Try again" lies wholly within the first 640px and that Discover's row starts at least 48px above its end; both fail on iteration 2's layout.

Record: at 360 by 640, "Try again" now spans 501px to 557px and Discover's row starts at 582px with its name on screen; at 320 by 568 "Try again" ends at 581px, so it still needs a short scroll there, behind the 177px header. The offline page's "Try again" moved from 434px to 387px. Desktop screenshots are unchanged. Iteration 4 is the frame's header on phones, then these pages reviewed again.

### System pages: iteration 4

The frame's header (`SiteLayout` in `apps/web/src/components/site-layout.tsx`), which every page draws, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 and 1280 wide.

Review:

- **The header took 177px of a phone's first screen**, iteration 3's open finding: the four site links wrapped onto two rows of their own under the wordmark and Account. They are 307px wide with no gaps, and a 320px screen leaves 288px, so no spacing change could fit them.
- **At 768px Account fell onto a second row** (a 125px header): from `md` the tagline joined the wordmark, and with the four links there was no room left for Account.
- The choice was fewer links on phones or a menu. A menu adds a tap before every link, needs a script or a disclosure widget the frame doesn't have, and puts Discover, the everyday way in, behind it. Fewer links costs nothing a visitor can't reach: the footer already lists all four at every width.
- Kept: the 48px link targets, the current page's underline, the offline page's header with no links, and desktop's layout.

Fix:

- Below 768px the header shows the wordmark, Discover, Polls and Account in one row; How it works and Moderation log are in the footer there. From 768px all four are in the header. The gaps tighten below 360px so the row fits at 320.
- The tagline beside the wordmark shows from 1024px, so the header is one row at 768.
- Tests: `HeaderPhone` checks at 360px that the header's links are Discover and Polls, that it is under 80px tall and that Account sits on the links' row (it fails on iteration 3's 177px header); the frame's story checks the header has two links below 768px and four above, and the footer four at every width.

Record: the header is 73px tall at every width measured, from 320 to 1280, where it was 177px on phones and 125px at 768. At 360 by 640 the error page's "Try again" now spans 394px to 450px (501px to 557px before) and Discover's row starts at 483px; at 320 by 568 "Try again" ends at 474px, so it is on the first screen at every size measured. Not found's Discover row starts at 475px at 360 by 640 (582px before), the offline page's "Try again" at 386px (387px). Desktop screenshots are unchanged. A phone visitor on How it works or the moderation log sees no current-page underline in the header, since its link is in the footer there: iteration 5 judges whether the page's title is enough.

### System pages: iteration 5

The three system pages and the frame, after iteration 4, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 and 1280 wide, light and dark, with How it works and the moderation log as the pages whose links a phone's header leaves out.

Review:

- **A phone named no current page on How it works or the moderation log**, iteration 4's open question. Their titles ("How a ballot stays secret, gets recorded and is counted in public", "Moderation acts on poll text, never on results") say what the page is about, which is enough for a sighted visitor, but neither repeats the link's words, and the footer, where both links are below 768px, marked no link as current at any width, so a screen reader user had no "current page" in either list.
- Kept: the 73px one-row header at every width, every page's action on the first screen (unchanged from iteration 4's measurements), the four places, the offline page's two links, both themes passing axe. Nothing else on the three pages would change.
- For the moderation log's own iterations (not these pages): at 320px its entries' 64-character IDs, truncated, still reach 335px, so the page scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- The footer's links take `aria-current="page"` as the header's do, drawn in semibold with the primary's 2px underline, so on a phone the footer says which page this is.
- Tests: the `Current` story checks the footer's Polls is current too; `CurrentPhone` at 360px on the moderation log checks that no visible header link is current and that exactly one footer link is, the moderation log's, in semibold (both fail on iteration 4's footer); the served pages' tests (`tests/ssr.test.ts`) expect the header's and the footer's links to `/polls` and `/how-it-works` as the only current ones.

Record: at 360 by 640 on the moderation log, the footer's "Moderation log" now stands out from the other three in weight and in its emerald underline; above 768px the header's link is marked as before and the footer's matches it. Every other screenshot is unchanged from iteration 4.

### System pages: iteration 6

The three system pages and the frame, after iteration 5, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 and 1280 wide, light and dark.

Review:

- **A phone scrolled through 136px of nothing before the footer**, iteration 2's finding for the frame, never taken up: the page's 40px bottom padding and the footer's 96px top margin, a fifth of a 640px screen. At desktop width the band sets the site's claim apart from the page; on a phone it is only scrolling, and on not found and the error page it sits between the last place to go and the footer's links.
- Kept: the 73px one-row header at every width; every page's action on the first screen (the error page's "Try again" at 394px to 450px and not found's Discover row at 475px at 360 by 640, unchanged); the four places; the offline page's two links; the footer's current page; both themes passing axe. The copy reads aloud without a stumble, and a first-time visitor on any of the three pages has one obvious thing to do.

Fix:

- Below 640px the footer's top margin is 48px (`mt-12`), so the band is 88px; from 640px it stays 96px.
- Tests: `FooterPhone` at 360px checks that the footer starts no more than 88px below not found's places (it was 136px, and the story fails on iteration 5's frame).

Record: at 360 by 640, not found's page is 1312px tall (1360px before) and the error page 1320px (1368px); the offline page's footer starts 48px nearer its "Try again". Every first-screen measurement and every desktop screenshot is unchanged from iteration 5.

### System pages: iteration 7

The three system pages and the frame, after iteration 6, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **Nothing on the pages would change.** Every measurement matches iteration 6's: the header is 73px tall at every width; at 360 by 640 the error page's "Try again" spans 394px to 450px and not found's Discover row starts at 475px; at 320 by 568 "Try again" ends at 474px; the offline page's "Try again" spans 386px to 442px; no page scrolls sideways at 320. The copy reads aloud without a stumble; each page says what happened in plain everyday words, then offers one obvious thing to do; the offline page's only links are "Skip to content" and "Try again"; the footer's band is 88px on a phone.
- **The error page had no dark story**, so axe had never judged its colours in the dark theme, where iterations 1 to 6 recorded "both themes passing axe" from not found's and the offline page's dark stories. Drawn dark, it uses the same tokens as those two (the primary button, the muted notes, the borders) and reads as they do.

Fix:

- `ErrorDark` runs the error page's story checks and axe in the dark theme (in both Storybook test projects); it passes, so nothing else changed.

Record: every screenshot is unchanged from iteration 6, and the error page in the dark theme shows the same layout on the near-black ground with the emerald "Try again". This is the system pages' fourth iteration in a row after the third, and the first to find nothing on them to change, so the surface is done. Home's iteration 1 is next.

### Home: iteration 1

The home page (`apps/web/src/components/home-page.tsx`, drawn by `src/routes/index.tsx`; stories `Components/Home page`, new in this iteration, inside the frame), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **The page spoke the civic register.** Its list was the poll register's rows: each poll named by the first 16 characters of its 64-character ID, a fingerprint, and mono "COUNTING" and "STATUS" labels, under "Listed polls" and a note about "polls this server lists, in order of their IDs" and "the election registry on chain". No row showed a question, so a visitor couldn't tell one poll from another or answer any of them. Discover's cards, built since, show the question and take an answer in a tap ([[language]], Everyday).
- **Its one action went to the register.** "Browse polls" opened `/polls`, the civic list, not Discover, the everyday way in. And under "Ask anything" there was no way to ask: the primary user is someone who wants an answer from a group ([[PROJECT_BRIEF]], Priority).
- **"Have a poll's ID?" took the right half of the first screen at desktop width**, a box that was two-thirds empty beside the title, for the 64-character ID that a visitor with a shared link never sees. The poll register keeps the same form.
- **On a small phone the action was below the first screen.** At 360 by 640 the 48px title took six lines and "Browse polls" spanned 641px to 697px; at 320 by 568, 669px to 725px.
- **The lede explained the mechanism** ("sealed on the voter's own device and posted to a public board, so anyone who downloads the board can count it again"), which the trust scene further down tells step by step; the first screen needs the promise in a visitor's terms.
- Kept: the title, "Non-binding, and run by citizens.", the trust scene, and a page that stays up, saying nothing of why, when the API doesn't answer (T-67).

Fix:

- Two ways in under the title: "Ask a question" (primary), to the account page, which signs a visitor in before the poll form (that form can't save without an account), and "Browse questions" (outline), to Discover. The ID lookup is left to the poll register.
- "What people are asking": the three newest open questions as the stream's own cards (`StreamCard`, checked and filtered as Discover's, T-68), one row at desktop width, each answerable from the card, with "See all questions" to Discover. The page asks the API for a three-poll page of the stream (`fetchStream` takes a `limit` up to its page of ten, T-52), not a register page of six, and says so when none are open or the API didn't answer.
- The title is 36px below 640px; the lede reads "Answering takes a tap or two, and every count is public, so nobody has to take anyone's word for a result."
- The register-rows component on its own (`PollRows`, its story kept) now that nothing draws the list's section; the home page's script budget is 170 KiB (165 before), for the cards' code beside the trust scene.
- Tests: the home stories check both ways in and where they go, three answerable cards, no ID form and no civic wording, the empty and unavailable states, and at 360px that both actions end within the first 640px (`Phone`); the served page (`tests/ssr.test.ts`) shows the stream's first three cards with their ballot links, asks for one listing of three (ten requests in all) and stays up without the API.

Record: before, the first screen at 360 by 640 held the frame, a six-line title and the lede, with "Browse polls" just under its edge, and the list below showed three rows of IDs with fingerprints; at 1280 the right half was the ID box. After, at 360 by 640 "Ask a question" spans 453px to 509px and "Browse questions" 521px to 577px; at 1280 both sit side by side at 517px and three question cards follow at 890px. At 320 by 568 "Ask a question" ends at 573px and "Browse questions" at 641px, so a short scroll is still needed there: iteration 2 judges the hero at that size, and whether the account page's sign-in is the right first step for "Ask a question".

### Home: iteration 2

The home page and the account page it sends askers to (`src/components/home-page.tsx`, `src/components/account-panel.tsx`, `src/routes/account.tsx`; stories `Components/Home page` and `Components/Account panel`), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **"Ask a question" ended on the account page**, iteration 1's open question. Signed out, the page said "Sign in, or create an account" and that an account "lets you create and manage polls", nothing about the question the visitor came to ask; once signed in, it showed the whole account: verification first, then "Polls you created" with its "Create a poll" button, below a phone's first screen. A visitor already signed in got the same. Linking home straight to the new poll's form (`/account/polls/new`) isn't better: signed out, the form only says so at its first save, after the question is written.
- **The second way in was below a small phone's first screen.** At 320 by 568 "Browse questions" spanned 585px to 641px; at 360 wide it ended at 577px, past a 568px-tall screen. The title is already iteration 1's 36px; the lede took five lines at 360 and six at 320.
- **The lede spoke of places** ("from your street to your county"), where the brief's primary user is a group: friends, a neighbourhood, a club, a classroom ([[PROJECT_BRIEF]], Priority). Its "Answering takes a tap or two" repeats what the cards below show.
- Kept: the title, both ways in and their order, the three answerable cards, "See all questions", the trust scene, the empty and unavailable states, both themes passing axe.
- For the stream card's own iterations (not this page): each card says "Closes January 8, 2026 at 12:00 AM UTC", a civic-register time for an everyday question; and at 768 the three cards stack one per row at the page's full width, so the section is nearly two screens tall.

Fix:

- "Ask a question" goes to `/account?next=new-poll`. There the account page titles itself "Sign in to ask your question" and says why a question needs an account and that the form opens next; once signed in (or if already), it loads `/account/polls/new` in its place without drawing the account. It is a full load, so the form's page keeps its own policy for Turnstile, and a replacement, so Back returns home. `next` takes that one value: the router redirects any other to the plain `/account`, so the page can't be made to send anyone elsewhere.
- The lede reads "For your friends, your street, your club or your class. Every count is public, so nobody has to take anyone's word for the result.", at 18px below 640px; there the hero's gaps are 16px and its top padding goes.
- Tests: `Phone` checks that both ways in end within 568px at 360 wide (iteration 1's ended at 577px); the account panel's `Asking` and `AskingDark` check the title and that signing in goes on without drawing the account, `AskingSignedIn` that a signed-in visitor goes straight on; the served pages (`tests/ssr.test.ts`) check home's link, the asking title on `/account?next=new-poll`, and the redirect for other values.

Record: at 360 by 640 "Ask a question" now spans 401px to 457px and "Browse questions" 469px to 525px (453px to 577px before); at 320 by 568 "Browse questions" ends at 561px, so both ways in are on the first screen at every size measured. At 1280 the actions moved up from 517px to 489px. From home, asking is now one tap and a passkey prompt to a blank question, where it was one tap, a prompt, a scroll and another tap. Iteration 3 reviews the page again, with the form as the asker's next screen.

### Home: iteration 3

The home page and the poll form an asker reaches from it (`src/components/home-page.tsx`, `src/components/trust-scene.tsx`, `src/components/step-scene.tsx`; stories `Components/Home page`, `Components/Trust scene` and `Components/Poll form`), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, scrolled through the trust scene on a phone as well as whole.

Review:

- **On a phone the trust scene hid each step's picture under that step's own card.** Below 1024px the scene's diagram stuck to the top of the screen (0px to 302px at 360 by 640) and the step cards scrolled over it, and a step becomes the drawn one when its card crosses the middle of the screen, so whenever a picture showed its step, the card was over its lower half: step 1's "What leaves your device" box with the lock sat behind its card, step 2's own "Yours" row was the only one of the board's rows left in sight. Each step took a 70svh band, 448px of a 640px screen, around a card of about 320px, and the page served without its script never moved past step 1's picture.
- Kept: the hero, both ways in (at 360 by 640 "Ask a question" spans 401px to 457px and "Browse questions" 469px to 525px, as in iteration 2), the three answerable cards and "See all questions", the trust scene's words, and its scrollytelling from 1024px, where the picture has its own column beside the cards. Both themes pass axe.
- **The asker's next screen, the poll form** (`NewPoll` at 360 by 640), titled "Create a poll" under a "Your polls" label where home's button said "Ask a question", offered "Back to your polls" to a first-time asker who has none, and its lede filled the first screen, so the question's own field began below it. These are the creating-a-poll surface's, and its iteration 1 takes them first; home's link to it is right.
- Still for the stream card's own iterations, from iteration 2: the closing time in UTC with a clock time, and one card per row at 768.

Fix:

- `StepScene` below 1024px draws each step's picture inside its card, under its words, with no sticky picture and no 70svh band, the cards 24px apart; from 1024px nothing changes. The pictures stay hidden from assistive technology.
- Tests: the trust scene's `Phone` story checks at 360px that the sticky picture isn't drawn and that each card shows its own step's picture below its heading (it fails on iteration 2's scene, which had no picture in any card); the step scene's story checks the sticky picture starts at the first step, that each card holds its own, and that only one of the two is drawn at the width the test runs; the mockups' scenes draw through the same `StepScene`, so the everyday home, poll, closed poll and place mockups' stories read the sticky picture, or the one drawn at the test's width, where they followed the scene. `/discover`'s script budget goes from 165 to 166 KiB: it was within a few bytes of 165, and the site's entry chunk, which every page loads, names home's route chunk by its hash, which this change moves, so its gzipped size moved by those bytes.

Record: at 360 by 640, each of the four steps now reads as one card, the words and then the picture (the answer and the lock, the board with "Yours", the 4 of 7 keys, the two counts with "They match."), and nothing scrolls over anything; the page is 5924px tall (5520px before), the extra 404px being the pictures that were hidden, in place of the blank bands. At 320 by 568 it is 6232px. At 1280 the page and its scrollytelling are unchanged (4404px). Iteration 4 reviews the page again.

### Home: iteration 4

The home page (`src/components/home-page.tsx`, with the stream card it draws, `src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Home page`), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **Every card said the same two sentences.** Under each card's tags, "Choose an answer to open it in the voting app. Nothing is sent until you cast your ballot there.", so a phone read it three times down the page and a screen reader three times in a row, while the section's lede above said "Answer any of them from here." The everyday home mockup says it once, beside the answers. The second card's note is the only one that says something of its own: that its answers are for question 1 of 2.

At 1280 the cards are 560px tall (600px), starting 28px lower under the two-line lede, and the page is 4392px (4404px). Iteration 5 reviews the page again.

### Home: iteration 11

The home page in every state it has (`src/components/home-page.tsx`; stories `Components/Home page`: `Default`, `Dark`, `Phone`, `Empty` and `Unavailable`), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, scrolled through as well as whole, its copy read aloud from top to bottom.

Review:

- **The public board's step promised what only the count can show.** Step 2 said "You keep a tracking code, so you can find yours there and see that it was counted", and the very next step says the count is "opened only after the poll closes". Read aloud in order, a first-time reader is told they can see their answer counted while the poll is still open, and then that nobody can count it until it closes. What the tracking code shows is that the sealed answer is on the board and stays there; a checked answer, or one replaced by a later answer, is on the board and never counted. How it works says the same thing correctly: "so you can check that it is there and stays there". The Spanish said "ver que se contó", the same promise.
- Kept: the title's three promises on their own lines from 640px, the hero and both ways in (at 360 by 640 they end at 457px and 525px; at 320 by 568 "Browse questions" ends at 561px, as in iterations 2 to 10), the lede naming the ballot app once over the cards, the empty and unavailable notes without it, "See all questions" after the cards where they stack and beside the title from 1024px, and the trust scene's pictures and layout as iterations 3 to 6 left them, with the trustee seats as iteration 10 named them. Step 4's "nobody has to take our word for it" echoes the lede's "nobody has to take anyone's word for the result" on purpose: the promise made at the top is the one the last step keeps. Both themes as before. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for the stream card's own iterations, from iterations 2, 9 and 10: the closing time in UTC with a clock time, one card per row at 768, the topic and place tags drawn as bordered boxes like the answers, and a card of several questions saying how many twice.

### Creating a poll: iteration 1

The poll form an asker reaches from home's "Ask a question" (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with the findings home's iteration 3 left it.

Review:

- **The page didn't answer the way in.** Home's button and the account's both lead here, home's saying "Ask a question", and the page's title said "Create a poll" under a "Your polls" label: a first-time asker, who has no polls, landed on a page named for a list they don't have, in words they hadn't chosen. The account's own button said "Create a poll" too.
- **"Back to your polls", twice, before anything is saved.** Under the lede and beside "Save draft", both to the account page, where a first-time asker has none. Before a first save there is no draft to go back to; the frame's Account link is there for an author who wants their list.
- **The lede filled a small phone's first screen.** At 360 by 640 the title, its four-line lede (150px to 318px) and the back link left the poll's Title field at 648px to 696px, below the first screen, and at 320 by 568 in the same place. The lede's first sentence ("Write your question and its answers, choose when it closes, and save it as a draft.") says what the four numbered sections below say again.
- Kept: the four numbered sections and their order, the preview beside the form from 1024px and after it where it stacks, the error summary, saving and submitting. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- For later iterations: the sections themselves, the preview's place on a phone (after the submit section, 6203px down at 360), and the submit section's review.

Fix:

- A new poll is titled "Ask a question" ("Hacer una pregunta") under "New poll" ("Nueva encuesta"); the account's button says "Ask a question" too. A saved draft keeps "Edit your poll" under "Your polls".
- "Back to your polls" is drawn only once the poll is saved, in both places.
- The lede is its promise alone: "Only you can see a draft, and you can change it as often as you like until you submit it."
- Tests: `NewPoll` checks the title and label, that no "Back to your polls" link is drawn, and that the Title field ends within 640px (it fails on the page before, where it ended at 696px); `WriteAndSave` checks that after the first save the page says "Edit your poll" and draws both links back. The keyboard pass in `tests/a11y.e2e.ts` judges a focused control's ring against the perimeter of its part on the screen: at 1280 by 800 the shorter header leaves the description field straddling the screen's bottom edge, where Chromium focuses it without scrolling, so its whole perimeter could never show; a control wholly on the screen is judged as before.

Record: at 360 by 640 the lede spans 150px to 234px and the Title field 516px to 564px, inside the first screen (648px to 696px before); at 390 by 844 the same; at 320 by 568 it starts at 544px (648px before), just under that first screen, which the frame's header would push further down anyway. The page is 6203px tall at 360 (6335px), 6540px at 320 (6684px), 5184px at 768 (5232px) and 4691px at 1280 (4739px). Iteration 2 reviews the page again.

The poll form after iteration 1 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with iteration 1's open findings: the sections, the preview's place on a phone and the submit section's review.

Review:

- **Who can answer was named by a code, with an account of it that no longer holds.** The section listed "Tier 0 (verified e-mail)", and the review's "Who can answer" row said the same, where the everyday register names a tier in words, never as a code ([[language]], Everyday). Its lede said "anyone with a voting identity on their device can answer, and anyone can make several", words an asker doesn't have, and since [[P2-31]] untrue as put: an answer needs a verified e-mail address (or, with the setting on, a phone number), and each counts once.
- **A new poll drew the whole review, and a button that could only refuse.** Before a first save, "Ready to ask?" went on into "Check it through" with "No title yet", "1 question", "Not set" and two "None" rows, 1,100px at 360 wide, then "Submit poll", which can only answer "Save your draft first". A first-time asker met two final actions, eight rows apart, for a draft that doesn't exist yet.
- **On a phone the preview came last**, iteration 1's open finding: under the submit button, 5,734px down a new poll at 360 and 7,724px down a saved draft, so an author reached "What people will see" only after the review and the submit button, if at all.
- Kept: the four sections' order, the fields and their help, the error summary, saving, the review's two groups and their "Change" links once there is a draft, the preview beside the form from 1024px. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- For iteration 3: the closing date and time are typed in UTC ("Closing date (UTC)", a six-line lede at 360 starting "Times are in UTC"), with the asker's own time shown only after, where an everyday asker means their own clock; the numbered section marks ("01 The poll") the everyday register leaves out ([[language]], Everyday); and at phone width each option's move and remove buttons wrap under its field, between the field and its details.

Fix:

- Who can answer, in the form and its review (T-01: the lede states the Sybil limit plainly), is "People with a verified e-mail address" ("… and phone number" with phone verification on), and the lede says what counts once: "For now, every poll is open to the same people. Each verified e-mail address counts once, so someone with two addresses could answer twice: results count addresses, not people." (numbers, with the setting on). Tiers 1 and 2 keep their names.
- Before a first save, the submit section is its title and lede and "Save your draft first. Then you can check it through here and submit it."; the review, the Cloudflare note and "Submit poll" are drawn once the draft is saved.
- The form's grid takes the preview between the fields and the submit section: where it stacks, it follows "Save draft"; from 1024px it is the second column across both, sticky as before.
- Tests: `NewPoll` checks there is no review or submit button before the first save and that the note says why; `WriteAndSave` that both are drawn after it; `TopicAndPlace` reads the review after saving, with the words for who can answer; `PhoneVerification` and `EmailVerification` check the words, what counts once, and that no "Tier 0" is shown; `PreviewBeforeSubmitPhone` at 360 checks the preview lies below "Save draft" and above the review (it fails on iteration 1's layout, where it came after both); `SubmitSavesFirst` starts from a new poll with no submit button.

Record: at 360 by 640 a new poll is 4,983px tall (6,203px before), with the preview at 4,139px to 4,568px, right after "Save draft" (3,987px to 4,043px), and the submit section at 4,709px ending in its note; at 320, 5,324px (6,540px); at 768, 4,320px (5,184px); at 1280, 3,819px (4,691px). A saved draft at 360 shows the preview at 6,029px to 6,941px, before "Ready to ask?" at 7,082px (it was at 7,724px, after "Submit poll"), and is 8,684px tall (8,676px); at 1280 it is unchanged at 6,221px. Iteration 3 reviews the page again, starting with the closing time.

### Creating a poll: iteration 3

The poll form after iteration 2 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with iteration 2's open findings: the closing time in UTC, the numbered section marks and the options' buttons on a phone.

Review:

- **The closing time was typed in UTC.** The fields said "Closing date (UTC)" and "Closing time (UTC)", and the section's six-line lede at 360 opened "Times are in UTC, the clock every poll keeps", so an asker in New York who means 10 in the morning had to work out 15:00 first; the time they meant was shown only afterwards ("That's … where you are"). The review row said "{when} UTC" alone.
- **The sections were numbered.** "01 The poll" to "05 Submit" opened each section in the civic record's `SectionMark`, which the everyday register leaves out ([[language]], Everyday), and each mark repeated its own heading ("When it closes" over "When does it close?"). The list of who can answer was drawn under a heavy rule, which the everyday register leaves out too.
- **On a phone each option's buttons wrapped under its field.** At 360 the move up, move down and remove buttons fell below the option's text field, between it and "Details for option 1", so the field, its buttons and its details read as three rows with nothing saying which the buttons belonged to.
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, the preview's place between saving and submitting, the review once there is a draft. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- For iteration 4: the When section's lede, now on the author's clock, is still six lines at 360; and the submit section's review on a phone.

Fix:

- The closing date and time are read on the author's own clock, the browser's time zone (`PollForm.timeZone`), and the draft still holds only the instant. The lede names the clock ("Choose a time on your own clock (Eastern Time)."), the fields are "Closing date" and "Closing time", and under them "People answering see it as … UTC, the clock every poll keeps." The review's row says both ("… your time, … UTC"); the preview keeps UTC, as people answering see it. A time the clock skips as it springs forward is named ("Your clock skips that time when it moves forward an hour. Choose another time."); a time it repeats as it falls back is read as its first pass. The conversion uses only `Intl` (`src/account/poll-form.ts`, `instantOf`).
- The form's sections are unnumbered: `Section` takes no `index` or `mark` in the everyday register, and the five marks' messages are gone. Who can answer is listed under the ordinary rule.
- Each option's buttons share its label's row, as a question's share its heading's; the field takes the row below at full width.
- Tests: `tests/poll-form.test.ts` reads dates and times in eight zones (New York, Berlin, Kolkata, St John's, Lord Howe's half-hour change, Kiritimati at UTC+14, and Apia's skipped day in 2011), names skipped times and reads repeated ones as their first pass, and checks by property that any instant shows a date and time that read back to it (or to its first pass), and any real date and time reads to an instant that shows it unless the clock skips it. `NewPoll` checks there is no section mark; `OptionControlsPhone` at 360 checks an option's three buttons lie above its field, on its label's row, and the field is as wide as its details (it fails on iteration 2's layout); `ClosesSkipped` types 2:30 on New York's spring-forward night and is refused with the words. The stories type 10:00 in New York for the 15:00 UTC they saved before. The poll form's script budget in `tests/ssr.test.ts` goes from 245 to 246 KiB (the conversion and the zone's name add about 0.4 KiB gzipped).

Record: a saved draft at 360 by 640 is 8,431px tall (8,684px), at 320, 9,124px (9,261px), at 768, 7,072px (7,089px), at 1280, 6,204px (6,221px); the new-poll story after its play, 5,282px at 360 (5,519px), 5,787px at 320 (5,968px), 4,707px at 768 (4,808px) and 4,206px at 1280 (4,307px). No section mark is drawn (five before). Known-weak: a draft saved by something other than this form at an instant in the second pass of a repeated hour reads back as the first pass, an hour earlier, and is saved so if the author saves again; the form itself never saves such an instant. Iteration 4 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 4

The poll form after iteration 3 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with iteration 3's open findings: the When section's lede and the submit section's review on a phone.

Review:

- **The When section's lede was seven lines at 360** (168px), and its middle explained setup's timing ("Your poll opens about half an hour after you submit it, once it is set up, and must then stay open for at least an hour, so …") to an asker who is choosing a time, not submitting. From 768px, where the lede sits beside the heading in five columns, it was eight lines and made the heading's row 200px tall. The timing is true at submission, not at the clock: a draft saved today can be submitted next week.
- **Each review row gave "Change" a row of its own on a phone.** Below 640px the row's label, its value and its 48px "Change" link stacked as three rows, so a one-line value took 130px and the review ran 1,252px at 360, nearly two screens between "Ready to ask?" and "Submit poll".
- **The Closes row said the date twice**: "November 4, 2026 at 10:00 AM your time, November 4, 2026 at 3:00 PM UTC", three lines at 360, where both clocks were on the same day.
- **The submit section's lede and the Cloudflare note ran together**, two paragraphs with no gap between them, so the note read as the lede's last lines.
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, the preview between saving and submitting, the review's two groups and their order, the closing time on the author's clock. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- The When lede is its rule alone: "Choose a time on your own clock (Eastern Time), from 90 minutes to a year from now." The setup's timing moves to the submit section's lede, where it applies: "… and it opens for answers when that's done, about half an hour later. It must then stay open for at least an hour. …" Spanish to match.
- Below 640px each review row puts "Change" on its label's row, at the row's end, with the value under both; from 640px the three columns are unchanged.
- The Closes row reads "{your time} your time ({UTC} UTC)", the UTC as its time alone where both clocks share the day, and with its date where they don't ("November 4, 2026 at 10:30 PM your time (November 5, 2026 at 3:30 AM UTC)").
- The submit section's two paragraphs are 12px apart.
- Tests: `ReviewPhone` at 360 checks that every "Change" lies on its label's row, the Closes row's single date, and that the When lede is at most three lines (it fails on iteration 3's page); `ClosesOnAnotherUtcDay` checks the row keeps both dates when the days differ (it fails on iteration 3's wording); `TopicAndPlace` reads the new Closes row.

Record: a saved draft at 360 by 640 is 8,227px tall (8,431px), its review 1,084px (1,252px), a one-line review row 102px (130px) and the When lede 72px, three lines (168px, seven); at 320, 8,896px (9,124px) with the review 1,108px (1,276px); at 768, 7,012px (7,072px) and at 1280, 6,144px (6,204px), where the When heading's row is 80px (200px). A new poll is 5,234px at 360 (5,282px), 5,715px at 320 (5,787px), 4,635px at 768 (4,707px) and 4,134px at 1280 (4,206px). Iteration 5 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 5

The poll form after iteration 4 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **From 640px each review row's "Change" read 12px under its label and value.** The row's three columns were top-aligned, and "Change" is centred in its 48px target, so its text sat on a line of its own, half a line below "Title" and "Harbor library hours" (at 1280, the label's middle at 5,457px, the link's at 5,469px).
- **"Submit poll" was the weaker of the page's two buttons on a saved draft.** Opened with nothing unsaved, the draft's one next step is submitting, but "Save draft", which then only saves what is already saved, kept the primary green and "Submit poll" was drawn in outline at the end of the review.
- **Mono marks over the preview and where it's listed.** "PREVIEW" sat over "What people will see" and only repeated it, and "WHERE IT'S LISTED" headed the listing's preview as an evidence label, which the everyday register keeps for hashes, entries and times ([[language]], Everyday: fewer mono labels).
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, the preview between saving and submitting, the review's two groups, the closing time on the author's clock, the preview's closing time in UTC (what people answering see). A question's move and remove buttons still take a row under its heading below 640px: the heading and three 48px targets need 265px, and a question's box leaves 246px at 360 and 206px at 320, so they can only share the row by shrinking the targets; unlike an option's (iteration 3), nothing comes between them and the question they move. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- From 640px the review row's columns share a baseline (`sm:items-baseline`), so "Change" reads on its label's line; below 640px the row is as iteration 4 left it.
- A saved draft with nothing unsaved draws "Submit poll" as the primary button and "Save draft" in outline; any edit gives the primary back to "Save draft" until it is saved again. A new poll is unchanged, with "Save draft" its one button.
- The preview has no mark over its heading, and "Where it's listed" is a heading (`h3`) in the page's own type, naming its list; the "Preview" message is gone.
- Tests: `ReviewDesktop` at 1280 (the preview's new `desktop` viewport) checks that each of the five "Change" links' text is centred within 1px of its label's line (it fails on iteration 4's page); `SubmitIsTheNextStep` checks that a saved draft's "Submit poll" carries the primary's colour and "Save draft" the outline's, that an edit swaps them and saving swaps them back, and that the preview shows no "Preview" mark and "Where it's listed" as a heading.

Record: at 1280 a saved draft's review rows keep their heights (74px for a one-line row) with each "Change" on its label's line; the page is 6,156px tall (6,144px), at 768, 7,008px (7,012px), at 360, 8,211px (8,227px) and at 320, 8,880px (8,896px). A new poll is 5,218px at 360 (5,234px), 5,699px at 320 (5,715px), 4,619px at 768 (4,635px) and 4,134px at 1280 (unchanged). On a saved draft the green button is now "Submit poll", under the review, and "Save draft" is outlined until the author edits. Iteration 6 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 11

The poll form after iteration 10 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll and a saved draft), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **Two options' problems read the same, word for word.** Saving the new poll as it opens lists "Question 1: Give this option its words, or remove it." twice, one link to each empty option, so someone reading the list can't tell which link fixes which option, and a screen reader's list of links reads two identical names going to two places (WCAG 2.4.4). Every problem inside an option, an empty, repeated, too long or unsavable option or option detail, named its question only.
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the problems 32px apart, the preview between saving and submitting at 360 and sticky beside the form from 1024px, the review's rows and their "Change" links, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll", the answer before what counts once. A question's move and remove buttons stay on their own row on a phone (iteration 10). Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- A problem in an option names the option as well as its question: "Question 1, option 2: Give this option its words, or remove it." (`pollFormInOption`, in both languages). `fieldErrors` gives each such problem its option, counting from 1 within its question; the field's own message, drawn by the option, stays as it was.
- Tests: `NewPoll` checks the two options' problems by their new words and that no two problems in the list read the same, `ProblemsInAQuestion` names the repeated option, and the unit test of `fieldErrors` gives each option's problem its option (all three fail on iteration 10's page).

Record: at 360 the refused new poll's list reads "Question 1, option 1: …" and "Question 1, option 2: …", each still two lines, so the page keeps its 5,298px; at 1280 each fits one line as before. Iteration 12 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 12

An option left alone in its question did the same.

### Creating a poll: iteration 14

The poll form after iteration 13 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll after a refused save, a saved draft and a draft being submitted), at 280 by 653, 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, and the review's who row measured at every width from 260px to 640px.

Review:

- **The review broke "e-mail" at its hyphen on the narrowest screens.** The review's values set `text-pretty`, which, to spare "address" a line of its own, ended the who row's first line at "People with a verified e-" and began the next with "mail address". It did so wherever that value was 212px to 270px wide: on the site, any screen under 303px, which is a folded phone's 280 and a 360px phone zoomed to 125% (288). The who section's own line, drawn without `text-pretty`, kept the word whole on every screen down to 280, so the one name read two ways down the same page.
- **Not a finding: the same break at 320 and 360 in this iteration's first screenshots.** They were taken with Storybook's dev page padded 24px on top of the story's own 24px, 48px narrower than the test runner and 32px narrower than the site, which put the row inside that band; with the dev page's padding removed, as iteration 13 says, the row is 312px wide at 360 in the test runner and 328px on the site.
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the move buttons only where a list has two or more, the problems 32px apart and each naming its option, the place's field and the topic's select at one height, the preview beside the form from 1024px, the review's rows and their "Change" links, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll". Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways, 280 included.

Fix:

- `wholeWords` draws each hyphenated word of our own words on one line (a `whitespace-nowrap` span), in the review's who row and the who section's list. An asker's text never goes through it, so a long hyphenated word they write still wraps.
- Storybook has a "Folded phone" viewport, 280 by 653, for stories about the narrowest screens.
- Tests: `WhoKeepsItsWordsFolded` checks at 280 that "e-mail" lies on one line in both places (it fails on iteration 13's page, two lines in the review); the stories reading the who-name match it across the span.

Record: at 280 the review's who row reads "People with a verified" over "e-mail address", still two lines, so no page's height changes; from 303px on the site, where the name already fit or broke at a space, nothing moves. Iteration 15 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 15

The poll form after iteration 14 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll after a refused save, a saved draft, and a draft being submitted and sent), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with the dev page's padding removed as iteration 13 says.

Taking you to its setup…", every field drops what is typed into it (the draft as saved is what goes to setup), and "Save draft" and "Submit poll" are disabled; but the review's five "Change" links stayed drawn as links, each scrolling to and focusing a field that then silently took no edits. A slow submission leaves the page in that state for as long as the request takes.
- **The review's questions were an indented list with no numbers.** The questions row's `ol` keeps the stylesheet reset's `list-style: none`, so under "2 questions" each question sat 20px in with nothing marking it, an indent that read as a stray, where the form above numbers the same questions "Question 1" and "Question 2".
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the move buttons only where a list has two or more, the problems 32px apart and each naming its option, the place's field and the topic's select at one height, "e-mail" whole on the narrowest screens, the preview beside the form from 1024px, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll", a new poll with no way back but the frame's (a first-time asker has no list to go back to). Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- Once the draft is sending or sent, the review draws no "Change": each row keeps the link's room, as the who row does, so no value moves.
- The questions row's list is numbered (`list-decimal`), "1." and "2." in its 20px indent.

### Creating a poll: iteration 16

The poll form after iteration 15 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a new poll, a saved draft, a draft being submitted and a refused save), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light, with the dark stories judged by axe and the dev page's padding removed as iteration 13 says.

Review:

- **The summary of a refused save didn't say which field was too long.** Two problems' words don't name their field, "This is too long." and "This holds a character that can't be saved. Retype it.", and the summary added only the question and option: a question's text and its explanation both read "Question 2: This is too long.", an option's words and its details both "Question 2, option 3: This is too long.", and the title's and the description's "This is too long." with nothing before it. Following the link finds the field, but the list itself, which a screen-reader user hears first, couldn't tell the author what to shorten.
- **A link in the poll's text pushed the page sideways.** The preview set `break-words` on the poll's own text, which breaks a long word only once the line is laid out, so a grid's column still takes the word's whole width: a description holding an 86-character link to the council's minutes made the preview's column, and the page, wider than a 360px phone, which then scrolled sideways (the stories' own check). The review's rows and the listing's place already used `wrap-anywhere`, which doesn't.
- Kept: the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the move buttons only where a list has two or more, the problems 32px apart, the place's field and the topic's select at one height, "e-mail" whole on the narrowest screens, the review's "Change" links gone once the draft is sent and its questions numbered, the preview beside the form from 1024px, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll". Both themes pass axe.
- For other surfaces' next iterations: the stream's card, the embed, the setup page, the account's polls and the flyer set `break-words` on an asker's text too; whether a long word widens them there depends on their layout, which this iteration didn't check.

Fix:

- Each text problem carries its field (`FieldError.field`), and the summary names it where the problem's words don't: "Title: This is too long.", "Description: …", "Place: …", "Question 2, explanation: …" and "Question 2, details for option 3: …", in both languages.
- The preview's title, description, questions, explanations, options and details break a long word anywhere (`wrap-anywhere`), so their width is never the word's.
- Tests: `TooLongNamesItsField` checks the summary's six lines for a title, description, question, explanation, option and details all too long, and `LinkInTheTextPhone` that a link in the description and an option's details keeps inside the preview at 360 with no sideways scroll (both fail on iteration 15's page); the form's checks name each text problem's field.

Record: at 360 the description's link wraps inside the preview, which grows by the lines it takes, and the page no longer scrolls sideways; a draft with no long word is unchanged at both widths (8,049px and 6,122px for the saved draft). Iteration 17 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 19

The poll form after iteration 18 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a saved draft and a new poll), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light, with the dark stories judged by axe, beside the ballot client's question screen (`apps/ballot/src/questions.tsx`).

Review:

- **The preview set a question's explanation as fine print.** "What people will see" drew "Until 9 PM." at 14px, smaller than the options under it (16px) and the smallest words on the question but the rule line. The ballot client people answer in sets the explanation at its options' size, under the question as part of its title, so the preview made an author's explanation look like a footnote they might cut or leave out.
- **The preview's options showed no kind of choice.** They were plain boxes, the same for "Choose one." as for "Choose up to 2.", where the ballot client draws a round mark for a question that picks one and a square one for a question that may pick more. An author changing the rule saw only the rule line change, so the preview didn't show at a glance what answering will look like.
- Kept: the preview's close in its own face and its emptied question's "No question yet" in the review; the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the move buttons only where a list has two or more, the problems 32px apart, each naming its option and field, the place's field and the topic's select at one height, "e-mail" whole on the narrowest screens, the review's description row, its "Change" links gone once the draft is sent and its questions numbered, a long word breaking anywhere in the preview, the preview beside the form from 1024px, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll". Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- The preview's question explanation is `text-base`, its options' size.
- Each of the preview's options draws the ballot client's empty mark, hidden from assistive technology (the rule line says it in words): round where an answer picks at most one, square where it may pick more, by the same test the ballot client uses (`maxSelections === 1`).
- Tests: `PreviewExplanationAtOptionsSizePhone` checks the explanation's size matches an option's, and `PreviewMarksTheChoice` that the saved draft's first question draws two round marks and its second three square ones, all hidden, and that choosing "Any number" for the first turns its marks square (both fail on iteration 18's page).

Record: at 360 the explanation reads "Until 9 PM." at the options' size and each option starts with an empty circle or square, the page 4px taller (8,162px to 8,166px in the story's frame); at 1280 it keeps its 6,195px. Iteration 20 reviews the page again.

### Creating a poll: iteration 20

The poll form after iteration 19 (`src/components/poll-form.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll form`, a saved draft, a new poll and a draft being submitted), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light, with the dark stories judged by axe, beside the ballot client's question screen (`apps/ballot/src/questions.tsx`, `answers.ts`).

Review:

- **The preview's rule line wasn't the ballot client's.** "What people will see" named the range in the form's own words: "Choose any number." for a question the ballot client introduces as "Choose as many as you like." when it can be left blank and "Choose at least one." when it can't, and "Choose 2 to 3." where it says "Choose from 2 to 3.". An "Up to" set to every option read "Choose up to 3.", where the ballot client says "Choose as many as you like.".
- **The preview said the opposite thing about skipping.** It added "You can leave this question blank." to a question that may be left blank, which the ballot client never says, and said nothing about one that can't be, where the ballot client says "This question can't be skipped.". So an author who left the checkbox unticked never saw what that tells people.
- Kept: the explanation at the options' size and the round or square mark by the kind of choice; the preview's close in its own face, the emptied question's "No question yet" in the review; the sections' order and fields, every section's heading row the heading alone with its lede under it, the move buttons only where a list has two or more, the problems 32px apart, each naming its option and field, the place's field and the topic's select at one height, "e-mail" whole on the narrowest screens, the review's description row, its "Change" links gone once the draft is sent and its questions numbered, a long word breaking anywhere in the preview, the preview beside the form from 1024px and held in view, the Cloudflare note by "Submit poll". Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- The preview's rule line is the ballot client's instruction for the question's range, read from its minimum, maximum and number of options as the ballot client reads them (`instructionOf`), in its words in both languages, followed by "This question can't be skipped." wherever an answer must choose at least one.
- Tests: `PreviewSaysWhatTheBallotSays` checks the saved draft's lines ("Choose one. This question can't be skipped." and "Choose up to 2."), then "Choose at least one." once question 1 is "Any number", "Choose as many as you like." with no note once it may be left blank, and "Choose from … to …" with the note for question 2 between two numbers (it fails on iteration 19's page); `TaggedDraftReadsBack` reads "Choose up to 2.".

Record: at 360 question 1's line reads "Choose one. This question can't be skipped." on two lines and question 2's "Choose up to 2." on one, so the saved draft keeps its height (8,464px in the story's frame) and its 6,227px at 1280. Iteration 21 reviews the page again.

### The author's list: iteration 1

The list of an account's own polls on the account page (`src/components/account-polls.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`: every state, in light and dark, the moderation history, the profile's choice per poll, an empty list and a list that couldn't load), at 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark.

Review:

- **At 768 the titles broke a word a line.** Each row was four columns from 768px (title, state, time, actions), and the actions' and the time's columns took what the title needed: "Should the harbor library open on Sundays?" stood 50px wide on eleven lines ("Shoul", "d the", "harb", "or"…), "Repaint the bandstand?" on four.
- **The columns didn't line up.** Each row sized its own columns, so from one row to the next the state and the time started at a different place (the states between 624px and 792px at 1280), and the page read as a scatter rather than a list.
- **A row's actions weren't one line or one kind.** "Edit draft" and "Follow its setup" were 36px links underlined in the text's colour; "Delete draft…", "Try again" and "Discard…" were 48px buttons underlined in green, so beside each other the link sat 6px higher, and the links were under the brief's 48px targets.
- **A closed poll still said "Open".** The list's state stays `open` after the close, so a poll closed a week ago read "Open" with "Closes" and a date already gone, where its own page says "Closed".
- Kept: the section's heading, its lede on who sees the list, "Ask a question" first, each state in words with its own icon and colour only on the icon, setup's steps as one state, the failures in plain words with what the author can do, the second click to delete or discard, focus back on the heading after an action, the moderation history and the profile's choice under each poll. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- For later iterations: a row with no action (a poll that can't open, an open poll whose text is hidden) is shorter than its neighbours; the lede beside the title from 768px, far from the list it describes; an empty list's one line.

Fix:

- From 768px the title has its row's whole width, and the state, the time and the actions sit under it in columns every row shares (10rem for the state, the rest for the time, the actions at the end); on phones the row stacks as before.
- Every link in the list is drawn as the buttons' link (`buttonVariants({ variant: "link" })`): 48px tall with the same green underline, so a row's actions share one line.
- An open poll past its close reads "Closed" ("Cerrada") with "Closed … UTC" ("Cerró el … UTC") and the circled check the poll pages use for a closed poll, judged by the clock as the poll page judges it (a ballot counts through `closes_at`'s second); its link to its page stays. The clock is a prop (`now`) so the stories read one day.
- Tests: `EveryState` and its dark story add a poll closed before the stories' day and check it says "Closed", never "Open", with its close and its page; `EveryStateDesktop` checks at 1280 that every title is its row's width, that the states and the times each start at one edge in every row, and that every link and button in a row is 48px tall. They fail on the list before (the closed poll read "Open").

Record: at 1280 the states all start at 40px and the times at 224px, and every title is on one line; at 768 the longest title is on one line where it took eleven; at 360 the rows stack as before, the drafts' "Edit draft" and "Delete draft…" now on one line. With the closed poll added, every state's story is 2,058px tall at 360, 1,419px at 768 and 1,395px at 1280 (each title's own row adds height on wide screens). Iteration 2 reviews the list again.

### The author's list: iteration 2

The list after iteration 1 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with the empty list, the moderation history, the profile's choice and the list that couldn't load.

Review:

- **Each poll's time was in the evidence's mark.** "Last edited October 11, 2026 at 2:20 PM UTC", "Submitted…", "Closes…" and "Closed…" were 12px monospace with wide tracking, the face [[language]] keeps for evidence (hashes, board entries), which creating a poll's iteration 18 had already taken off the preview's close. A time here is a plain fact about the author's own poll, and at 360 every one of them broke mid-time, "2:20" over "PM UTC".
- **A row with no action was shorter than its neighbours.** From 768px the state, the time and the actions share one line, and the actions' 48px set its height; a poll with nothing to do ("Move the market to Saturdays?", registered but never opened) drew that line 20px tall, so its state sat 14px nearer its title than every other row's. Iteration 1 noted it for later.
- Kept: the title on its own line, the shared columns from 768px, every action 48px and drawn as the buttons' link, a closed poll reading "Closed", the state in words with colour only on its icon, the failures' plain words with what the author can do, the section's heading and lede (the account page's sections all set their lede beside the heading from 768px, so it changes there or nowhere), "Ask a question" first, the empty list's one line under the rule (the button above it is the way on). Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

Fix:

- Each time is `text-sm` in the body's face and muted text, the state's own size, as the poll's page and the preview state a close.
- From 768px the actions' cell is at least 48px tall whether or not it holds an action, so every row's line is one height.

### The author's list: iteration 4

(and every failure that leaves a poll registered: the registration's or the opening's mismatch, an opening past the close) ends its reason "To ask your question, start a new poll." and its row had no action at all. - Kept: the title on its own line, the shared columns from 768px with every row's line one height, the reason before its actions, every action 48px and drawn as the buttons' link, times in the body's face, a closed poll reading "Closed", the section's heading and lede, "Ask a question" first, the empty list's one line, the moderation history and the profile's choice under each poll. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- For later: "Closed" then "Closed October 17, 2026…" says the word twice, aloud and to the eye; the time's own word is what makes it a sentence on a phone, where it stacks under the state, so it stays until a better line is found.

Fix:

- A poll whose failure leaves it registered offers "Start a new poll" ("Crear una encuesta nueva"), the reason's own words, linking to the creation form, after the reason as every row's actions are.
- A closed poll offers "See its results" (`/polls/<id>/results`) before "Go to its page". An open poll keeps only its page: the results section there says when results appear.

They fail on iteration 3's list, where neither link exists.

Record: at 1280 and 768 nothing moved but the two new links on their rows' state lines ("See its results" beside "Go to its page", "Start a new poll" alone at the end of the registered poll's line); the story keeps its heights (1,423px at 1280, 1,447px at 768). - **The moderation history still broke its times.** Under a poll moderation acted on, the decision and each appeal state their times in the rows' words ("Text hidden: Personal data, October 16, 2026 at 8:00 AM UTC", "You appealed decision No. 47 on October 14, 2026 at 12:00 PM UTC: closed on October 16, 2026 at 8:00 AM UTC…"), but iteration 6's no-break spaces were only the rows' own. It fails on iteration 6's list (a time in two boxes). The moderation stories match the decisions' accessible names with `\s` at the times' spaces, since an accessible name keeps a no-break space as it is.

Record: at 360 the hidden poll's decision reads "October 16, 2026 at 8:00 AM UTC" with the time whole on its second line, and its first appeal wraps at "at", with "8:00 AM UTC, because a later decision" on the next line; the restored poll's appeal ends "3:00 PM UTC, and the poll restored." on one line. The list after iteration 9 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640, 768 by 1024 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the empty list, the list that couldn't load, the moderation history and each poll's profile choice.

Review:

- **A poll that hadn't opened was told its profile showed it.** Under every row the profile's box read "Your profile lists this poll, and its page says your profile asked it, while the poll is listed", and ticking it on a draft answered "Your profile shows this poll." But the public profile lists only polls whose election is registered ([[0040-creator-profiles]]; `packages/core/src/profiles.ts`), so a draft, a poll in setup or one that failed and can be tried again isn't on it, and an author who opened their profile to check would find it missing. The form's box on a saved draft said the same; only a new poll's, before its first save, said "once the poll is open and listed".
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 9 left. The title on its own line; the shared columns from 768px, every row's line one height; the reason before its actions; every action 48px and drawn as the buttons' link; times in the body's face and wrapped whole, in the moderation history too; "Closed" then "On …"; "See its results" before "Go to its page"; "Start a new poll" on a poll that can never open; the lede naming every poll the list holds; "Ask a question" first; the empty list's one line right under the button; a list that couldn't load saying so where the list would be, with the way to load it again; an action's outcome under the heading focus returns to. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.

- `ProfileChoice` takes `unopened`: its help reads "Your profile lists this poll, and its page says your profile asked it, once the poll is open and listed. Untick it at any time." and a tick answers "Your profile will show this poll once it opens." ("Tu perfil mostrará esta encuesta cuando se abra."). The list passes it for a draft, a poll in setup and a failed poll that can be tried again; an open or closed poll, and one that failed with its election on the record, keep the words they had, since the profile can list them. The form passes it on a saved draft.
