### The author's list: iteration 13

The list after iteration 12 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and the moderation history it draws, `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history, an appeal and each poll's profile choice.

Review:

- **At desktop width each appeal ran the row's whole width.** "You appealed decision No. 47 on October 14, 2026 at 12:00 PM UTC: closed on October 16, 2026 at 8:00 AM UTC, because a later decision changed the poll first." was one line 1,182px wide at 1280, about 150 characters, right under the decision's meaning held to the prose measure (526px) like every other sentence in the list. The eye had to travel the whole row and find its way back.
- **The moderation history's actions weren't the row's.** "See the moderation log" was a plain underlined line of text, 14px tall, in the text's colour, where every action in the row above it ("Go to its page", "Edit draft") is a 48px target drawn as the buttons' link; "Appeal this decision…" was the buttons' link with its height taken away (`h-auto`). The two kinds of link side by side read as two kinds of thing, and the history's were the hardest to tap on a phone.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 12 left. The title on its own line; the shared columns from 768px, every row's line one height; the reason before its actions; every row's action 48px and drawn as the buttons' link; times in the body's face and wrapped whole, in the moderation history too; "Closed" then "On …"; "See its results" before "Go to its page"; "Start a new poll" on a poll that can never open; the lede naming every poll the list holds; "Ask a question" first; the empty list's one line right under the button; a list that couldn't load saying so where the list would be, with the way to load it again; an action's outcome under the heading focus returns to; a tick on a poll that hasn't opened answering when the profile will show it; what the boxes do, and the profile's name, said once above the list. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7).

Fix:

- Each appeal keeps the prose measure (`max-w-prose`, `text-pretty`), as the decision's meaning above it does.
- "Appeal this decision…" and "See the moderation log" share one line drawn as the row's links (`buttonVariants({ variant: "link" })`, 48px), wrapping onto two where the row is narrow, as the row's own actions do.
- Tests: a new `ModerationHistoryDesktop` story checks every appeal is no wider than the decision's meaning and each "See the moderation log" is the height of the row's "Go to its page"; `AppealADecision` checks the appeal's button and the log's link are each at least 48px tall. Both fail on iteration 12's code.

Record: at 1280 each appeal now wraps at 526px, two lines where it was one, under the meaning's edge, and "See the moderation log" is drawn as "Go to its page" is, green-underlined in a 48px line; the moderation-history story is 1,350px tall where it was 1,234. At 360 the appeals read as before and each log link takes a 48px line (1,917px where it was 1,861). Iteration 14 reviews the list again.

### The author's list: iteration 14

The list after iteration 13 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history, an appeal and each poll's profile choice, measured in the browser.

Review:

- **The list's rule sat 32px under "Ask a question", not 16px.** The status line that announces an action's outcome is empty until there is one, and its `empty:-mt-4` was meant to take back the grid's gap. A negative margin doesn't shrink a grid track: the empty line still drew 16px tall, so the button, the empty line and the rule were two gaps apart, and the list looked loose from its own button on every visit.
- **Each moderation history's rule ran 12px past its last line.** Its own empty status line, last in a `gap-3` grid, added one gap under "See the moderation log", so the left rule ended in a blank band under the link, longer than the space the link's 48px target already leaves.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 13 left. The title on its own line; the shared columns from 768px, every row's line one height; the reason before its actions; every action 48px and drawn as the buttons' link, the moderation history's too; appeals at the prose measure; times in the body's face and wrapped whole; "Closed" then "On …"; "See its results" before "Go to its page"; "Start a new poll" on a poll that can never open; the lede naming every poll the list holds; "Ask a question" first; the empty list's one line right under the button; a list that couldn't load saying so where the list would be, with the way to load it again; an action's outcome under the heading focus returns to; what the boxes do, and the profile's name, said once above the list. Both themes pass axe. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results (`account-follow-results.tsx`) draws its empty status line with the same `empty:-mt-4`, for that list's own iterations.

Fix:

- Both status lines are taken out of the grid while empty (`empty:absolute`): they stay in the accessibility tree, so an outcome is still announced when its words arrive, and they take no track and no gap until then.
- Tests: `EveryStateDesktop` checks the list's rule is 16px under "Ask a question"; `ModerationHistoryDesktop` checks each history ends at its "See the moderation log". Both fail on iteration 13's code.

Record: at 1280 the list's rule is now 16px under the button (32px before), and each moderation history ends with its log link's 48px line, 12px shorter; the moderation-history story is 1,310px tall where it was 1,350 at 1280, and 1,877px where it was 1,917 at 360; every state's story is 2,106px at 360 and 1,383px at 1280. Iteration 15 reviews the list again.

The account's report form ([[P2-27]], `report-section.tsx`) already moves focus to its question when it opens and back to its button when it closes; these three questions didn't.

- The block says "This poll's moderation couldn't be loaded just now." ("No se pudo cargar la moderación de esta encuesta en este momento."), the words the list uses, with "Load its moderation again" ("Volver a cargar su moderación") under it, a 48px button drawn as the list's links and described by the poll's title. It reads that poll's moderation alone; the failure stays on screen until the answer comes, so the block never vanishes with focus in it, and focus then goes to the block's heading, as after filing. Loaded again with nothing ever decided, the block says "Moderation hasn't decided anything about this poll." ("La moderación no ha decidido nada sobre esta encuesta.") rather than disappearing under the author's focus.

### The author's list: iteration 24

The list after iteration 23 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history, filing an appeal, a list that couldn't load, a poll whose moderation couldn't load, the profile's choice and an empty list.

Review:

- **Two reasons a poll didn't open named its trustees.** Under "Repaint the bandstand?" the row read "The poll's trustees couldn't finish preparing its keys after four tries.", and a poll refused at registration "The trustees' work on your poll didn't pass our checks". Trustees are invisible infrastructure in an everyday poll ([[PROJECT_BRIEF]], Non-negotiable properties, threshold trust; [[language]], Two registers: they belong to the evidence one step down), and an author who never chose any reads "the poll's trustees" as people they should have picked or can contact. Nothing else in the list names them, and the author can do nothing about them but what the reason already offers.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 23 left, including the moderation block's "Load its moderation again". The screenshots showed nothing else to change in either theme.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22 and 23), for the profile's iterations; and the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (`setupLede`, `setupStep_keyed`), for creating a poll's iterations.

Fix:

- The reasons say what failed, not who: "Making the key that seals your poll's answers didn't finish after four tries." ("La clave que sella las respuestas de tu encuesta no se terminó de crear tras cuatro intentos.") and "Your poll's setup didn't pass our checks, so we didn't register it." ("La preparación de tu encuesta no pasó nuestras comprobaciones, así que no la registramos."), the rest of each as before. The setup page's failure shows the same words.

### The author's list: iteration 25

The list after iteration 24 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history and filing an appeal, its words read aloud.

Review:

- **The appeal of the decision a block shows came last.** Under "Rename the east pier?" the block's heading is decision No. 52, "Text hidden: Personal data", then what it means, and then the first appeal read "You appealed decision No. 47 on October 14…: closed…, because a later decision changed the poll first." No. 47 appears nowhere else in the block, so read straight after No. 52's meaning, the closed appeal reads as this decision's; the line that answers "did my appeal of this work?" ("You appealed decision No. 52…: rejected…") was the block's last, four lines further down at 360. The server sends the appeals oldest decision first, and the block drew them in that order, while its heading is the newest decision.

- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22 and 23), for the profile's iterations, which lists its appeals in the same order; and the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (iteration 24), for creating a poll's iterations.

Fix:

- The history lists its appeals newest decision first, so the appeal of the decision in the heading is right under its meaning and older decisions' follow. The server's order and the account export's are unchanged.
- Tests: the moderation history's stories (`ModerationHistory` and its dark, desktop, phone and folded ones) check that the hidden poll's appeals read No. 52, then No. 47. They fail on iteration 24's order.

### The author's list: iteration 26

The list after iteration 25 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history and filing an appeal, following each of the block's links.

Review:

- **The log link opened the log at its start, not at the decision.** Under "Rename the east pier?" the block names decision No. 52 and the appeal of No. 52, then "See the moderation log", which went to `/moderation`: the log's first 50 decisions, oldest first. No. 52 is on the second page, reached by "next" with nothing saying it is there; on a running deployment a poll's decision is hundreds of pages in. The author who came to check what was decided, or to show it to someone, couldn't find it from the link meant for that.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 25 left, including the appeals newest decision first. The screenshots showed nothing else to change in either theme.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22, 23 and 25), for the profile's iterations, whose log link also opens at the start, as does the poll page's moderation note's; the log's page itself, whose first row comes after its limits and grounds, for the log's iterations; and the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (iteration 24), for creating a poll's iterations.

Fix:

- A block with a decision links to `/moderation?after=<its number − 1>`, so the log's first row is the decision the heading names, and says so: "See it in the moderation log" ("Verla en el registro de moderación"). A block with no decision to show (a failure alone) keeps "See the moderation log" at the log's start. The log's route, its paging and the API are unchanged.
- Tests: `ModerationHistory` and its dark, desktop, phone and folded stories check the hidden poll's link opens at No. 52 (`after=51`) and the restored one's at No. 60 (`after=59`); `AppealADecision` checks No. 41's (`after=40`). They fail on iteration 25's code (the link to `/moderation`). The account page's script budget in `tests/ssr.test.ts` goes from 197 to 198 KiB: the new words in both catalogs took it 12 bytes over.

Record: at 360 the link is the same one line, six characters longer; nothing else moved, in either theme. Iteration 27 reviews the list again.

### The author's list: iteration 27

The list after iteration 26 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx` and `src/components/poll-moderation.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state and the moderation history, following each row's links.

Review:

- **A poll that can't open sent its author to an empty form.** Under "Move the market to Saturdays?" the reason ended "To ask your question, start a new poll." and the row's "Start a new poll" opened `/account/polls/new`: a blank title, one blank question and two blank options. The author's title, description, every question and every option are still theirs on the server (the drafts handler's read answers any of the author's own polls, whatever its state), but the only way to ask the question again was to type all of it again. Three failures end this way (`registration-mismatch`, `open-mismatch`, `open-timeout`), all after the author has already been through setup once.
- **The same action had two names.** The list's own button, home's way in and the form's title say "Ask a question"; the row and its reason said "Start a new poll".
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 26 left, including the log link opening at the block's decision. The screenshots showed nothing else to change in either theme.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22, 23, 25 and 26), for the profile's iterations; the log's page itself (iteration 26), for the log's iterations; the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (iteration 24), and its failure for a poll that can't open, which says the question can be asked again but links only "Back to your polls", for creating a poll's iterations.

Fix:

- The row's link is "Ask it again" ("Volver a hacerla"), described by the poll's title, to `/account/polls/new?from=<its ID>`. The three reasons end "You can ask your question again as a new poll, starting from what you wrote." ("Puedes volver a hacer tu pregunta en una encuesta nueva, a partir de lo que escribiste.").
- The poll form takes `from` on a new poll (the route's `?from=`, a poll ID or nothing): it reads that poll through the author's own session and fills the new form with its words, topic, place and who can answer, but not its closing time, which was chosen for a poll that never opened and has often passed. Its lede says so: "Your words from the poll that didn't open are filled in. Choose when this one closes; only you can see it until you submit it." The first save creates a new draft, checked by the form's rules as any other (T-34, T-72); the failed poll is never changed. A `from` that can't be read starts an empty form, which is what the link promised.
- Tests: `EveryState` checks the row's link, its address and that it comes after the new reason; `AskAgain` (poll form) checks the words come back, the closing time is empty, the lede, and that saving creates a new draft equal to the old one's; `AskAgainFromAPollGone` checks an unreadable poll starts an empty form with no alert. They fail on iteration 26's code (the old link, and a form ignoring `from`).

Record: at 360 and 1280 the registered poll's row is the same height, its link "Ask it again" where "Start a new poll" was; the reason is one line longer at 360. Following it, the form opens filled in, with the closing date and time empty. Iteration 28 reviews the list again.

### The author's list: iteration 28

The list after iteration 27 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state, the moderation history and the profile's choices, following each row's actions.

Review:

- **"Try again" didn't try again.** Under "Repaint the bandstand?" the reason ended "You can try again or discard it." and the row offered "Try again". Pressing it starts nothing: it puts the poll back to its draft (core's `retryFailedPoll` clears what setup wrote, and the next submission is a new election), and the list said "Your poll is a draft again, as you wrote it." with the row now a draft. Read aloud, the button and the reason promise setup starting over; what the author got was a draft still to be found on the list, opened ("Edit draft") and submitted, three more taps, with nothing saying it must be. Two of the reasons that end on the button say "Choose a later closing time and submit it again" and "Change who can answer and submit it again", which only the draft's form can do.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 27 left, including "Ask it again" for a poll that can't open. The screenshots showed nothing else to change in either theme.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22, 23, 25 and 26), for the profile's iterations; the log's page itself (iteration 26), for the log's iterations; the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (iteration 24), and its failure for a poll that can't open (iteration 27), for creating a poll's iterations; and how many have answered each open poll, which the poll page and the register now show (P2-19 parts 17 and 21) but the list has no read for, a change to the drafts handler rather than this list's words.

Fix:

- The button is "Edit and submit again" ("Editar y volver a enviar"). Once the server has put the poll back to its draft, the list opens that draft (`/account/polls/<id>`, the `onRetried` prop's default), where it is checked, changed if its reason says so, and submitted; the list stays busy while the page leaves, so nothing is pressed twice. A refusal stays on the list and says why, as before. The status "Your poll is a draft again, as you wrote it." is gone with nothing left to say it.
- The ten reasons that ended "You can try again or discard it." end "You can submit it again or discard it." ("Puedes volver a enviarla o descartarla."), `electorate-empty`'s "You can submit it again later or discard it.", and the setup page's link after such a failure reads "Back to your polls, to submit it again or discard it".
- Tests: `RetryAFailedPoll` checks the reason's words, that the button opens the draft with the poll's ID after the one retry call, and that both of the row's buttons are disabled meanwhile; `RefusedAsRegistered` checks a refusal opens nothing; the every-state stories find the new button; `OutcomeAboveALongList` checks the outcome's place after a discard, the action left with a status line; the setup story checks its link's words. They fail on iteration 27's code (the old words, and a retry that stays on the list).

Record: at 360 and 1280 the every-state story is the same height (2,126px at 360); "Repaint the bandstand?" reads "Edit and submit again" and "Discard…" on one line, and its reason is the same three lines. Iteration 29 reviews the list again.

### The author's list: iteration 29

The list after iteration 28 (`src/components/account-polls.tsx`; stories `Components/Account polls`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every state and the moderation history, its words read as the author who has just submitted a poll reads them.

Review:

- **A poll in setup was told "You can leave this page" where there is no page to leave.** Under "Which crossing should get a signal first?" the reason read "Setting up takes a while, mostly waiting for the public record to settle. You can leave this page, or follow its setup step by step." Those are the setup page's words (its `setupLeave`: "This page checks again every few seconds. You can leave it…"), where the author watches the steps go by; on the list nothing is being watched, so "this page" is the account page and leaving it means nothing. What an author who has just submitted wants to know here is whether anything more is needed from them and how they will find out it opened: setup opens the poll on its own (core's opening step, `poll-opening.ts`), no notice says so, and this list is where its opening or its failure shows.
- Kept: everything iterations 1 to 28 left, including "Edit and submit again" opening the draft. The screenshots showed nothing else to change in either theme.
- Still for later, not this list's alone: midnight read as "12:00 AM UTC" (iteration 7); the account page's list of polls with results draws its empty status line with `empty:-mt-4` (iteration 14); the profile's own moderation (iterations 15 to 17, 22, 23, 25 and 26), for the profile's iterations; the log's page itself (iteration 26), for the log's iterations; the setup page's lede and its step "The trustees make the key that seals answers" (iteration 24), and its failure for a poll that can't open (iteration 27), for creating a poll's iterations; and how many have answered each open poll (iteration 28), a change to the drafts handler.

Fix:

- The reason reads "Setting up takes a while, mostly waiting for the public record to settle. Nothing more is needed from you: it opens on its own, and this list shows when it does, or why it didn't. You can also follow it step by step." ("…No necesitas hacer nada más: se abre sola, y esta lista muestra cuándo se abre, o por qué no se abrió. También puedes seguirla paso a paso."). The link "Follow its setup" is unchanged.
- Tests: the every-state stories check the new words on the poll in setup and that "leave this page" is gone. They fail on iteration 28's words. The longer words fit the pages' script budgets in `tests/ssr.test.ts` as `main` now sets them.

Record: at 360 the every-state story is 40px taller (2,126px to 2,166px), the setup reason two lines longer; at 1280, 20px taller, one line. Nothing else moved, in either theme. Iteration 30 reviews the list again.

### The stream and its card: iteration 1

`/discover` and its card (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 360px and 1280px, light and dark).

Review:

- **At 360px a phone's first two screens held no question.** Above the first card sat the title and lede, the search box, the place box, the search button, the three orders and their note, and fifteen topic links in six rows with their note: the first card's heading began about 1,400px down, and at 1280px about 1,000px down, under an 800px window. The stream's point is the questions; the fifteen topics and the place are for the visitor who already knows what they want narrowed.
- **Every card said the same two sentences**: "Choose an answer to open it in the ballot app. Nothing is sent until you cast your ballot there." three times on the story's page, once per card, though the card already has `explained` for a page that says it once above (the home page does).
- **The search hint said "Results keep the order above"**, but the orders are below the box.
- **The search button sat alone on its own row** under the place box, so at 360px a search was a box, two hints, a second box and then the button.
- Kept: each order and topic a plain link and each form a plain GET, so the page works before its script loads; the typed search and place shown only in their boxes, never in our words (T-67); counts per tier, never summed (T-01); one question per card, its answers each a way into the ballot client.
- For a later iteration: each card's closing time reads "January 8, 2026 at 12:00 AM UTC" and wraps at 360px (the same midnight-in-UTC reading noted for the author's list); at 1280px the card's top line spreads "Asked by", "Closes" and "3 questions" to the card's two edges and middle; the lede is six lines at 360px.

Fix:

- The topics and the place are folded under one native disclosure, "Topic and place", closed unless the page is narrowed by a topic or a place (or the place was refused), so it opens before any script and shows what narrows the page. The place has its own GET form with its own "Find" button, and each form keeps the other's accepted value, the order and the topic as hidden fields, as the links keep them.
- The search button sits beside its box.
- The stream says once, above the cards, what choosing an answer does (the home page's sentence), and each card is `explained`: a poll of several questions still says "These answers are for question 1 of 3."; with no ballot client neither shows.
- The hint says "Results keep the order below." (and in Spanish, "el orden de abajo").
- The stories check the disclosure closed on the default page and open on a topic's, each form's hidden fields, the place's refused value kept in its box, and the one explanation; the SSR test checks the explanation and the per-card line.

Record: before, the first card began about 1,400px down at 360px; after, about 940px down, with the disclosure's one line in place of seven rows of topics and the place's box, and each card about 60px shorter without its repeated sentences.

### The stream and its card: iteration 4

The stream after iteration 3 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, and in Spanish), read as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`.

Review:

- **"Most answered this week" still took a row of its own at 360px**, iteration 3's open finding: the three orders wrapped as whole links, so they spent two 48px rows and a gap (104px) on three choices. Shortening the label alone couldn't fit them; letting each label wrap inside its own box can.

Someone browsing questions knows a site, not a server or a definition (as the system pages' iteration 2 found), and the ID told them nothing.
- Kept: the disclosure's fold, the five-line lede, the one explanation above the cards, the search button beside its box, each count's two clean lines at 360px, the card's top line kept together at 1280px, times that keep "UTC" whole, counts per tier and never summed (T-01), the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. Fix:

- Below `sm` the three orders share one row: each takes what its label needs, the longest narrowing most, and its label wraps centred inside its own 48px-high box ("Most answered" over "this week" at 360px). From `sm` up they are as before.

Its own page has everything we hold about it.", or that its details came through damaged, or belong to a different poll, so its question and answers can't be trusted here (in Spanish too). Under that, a small "Poll 4040404040404040…" in monospace describes the link: two such cards' links share their words, and the ID's start is all that tells them apart to a screen reader (the a11y e2e's link-purpose check). The poll register keeps its own wording.
- Tests: the stream's stories check that the three orders share one row with no label overflowing (at the phone's 360px and at desktop width, failing on iteration 3's layout at 360px), that the first card's answers and count are at most 576px wide (failing on iteration 3's at 1280px), and that the unreadable card says nothing of a "server" and its link is described by the ID's start; `Unreadable` checks that two such links have their own descriptions, and the damaged and the other-poll wording, `SpanishOrders` the orders on one row and the card's words in Spanish.
- The new words in the shared catalog take home's first-visit script 103 bytes past its budget, which rises from 178 to 179 KiB.

The stream after iteration 4 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover` and as one narrowing to a topic.

Review:

- **At 1280px each card ran the page's whole width, about 1,200px**, while iteration 4 kept its answers and count to 576px: every card's right half was empty, and the eye travelled from a short question at the left to a border at the far right. The search form above is at most 672px wide, so the cards were the only thing on the page that ran edge to edge.
- **The "Topic and place" line's focus ring touched its own contents**: the summary had no padding, so the ring ran along the chevron at the left and the "e" of "place" at the right, in both themes and at both widths.
- Kept: the disclosure's fold, the five-line lede, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, the search button beside its box, each count's two clean lines at 360px, the card's top line kept together, times that keep "UTC" whole, the unreadable card in everyday words, counts per tier and never summed (T-01), the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), a civic-register time for an everyday question, shared with every surface that draws `When`; and on a phone a card's top line (who asked, the closing time on two lines, the question count) can take four lines above its question.

Fix:

- The stream's section of questions, its empty states and "More questions" is at most 768px wide (`max-w-3xl`); a phone is unchanged, and the home page's three-up row of cards doesn't use it.
- The summary is padded 8px each side and pulled back by as much (`-mx-2 px-2`), so its chevron keeps the column's edge and its ring clears both ends.
- Tests: the stream's stories check that the first card is at most 768px wide (failing on iteration 4's layout at desktop width) and that the summary has its 8px padding.

Record: before, at 1280 by 800 each card was about 1,200px wide, its answers ending 641px from the page's left edge and its border 1,240px; after, the card is 768px wide and its border ends 808px from the left, with the answers still 576px wide inside it. The line above the cards now wraps to two lines at 1280, so the first card's heading begins 792px down instead of 768px. At 360px nothing moves: the first card's heading still begins 939px down. The summary's chevron stays at the column's edge, and its ring now sits 8px clear of the chevron and of "place".

### The stream and its card: iteration 6

The stream after iteration 5 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, and in Spanish), read as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`.

Review:

- **On a phone a card's top line took up to four lines above its question**, iteration 5's open finding: "Asked by Riverside Neighbours", then "Closes January 15, 2026 at" over "12:00 AM UTC", then "3 questions". Every card's closing time took two lines at 360px, since the long month leaves the clock no room on the first.
- **"3 questions" was said twice on one card**: in the top line and, a few lines down, in the note above the answers ("These answers are for question 1 of 3."), which a card offering answers always shows for a poll of several questions.
- Kept: the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold and its ring's room, the five-line lede, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, each count's two clean lines at 360px, times that keep their clock and "UTC" whole, the unreadable card in everyday words, counts per tier and never summed (T-01), the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), a civic-register time for an everyday question, shared with every surface that draws `When`.

Fix:

- The card's closing time abbreviates its month (`When`'s `short`, the locale's medium date): "Closes Jan 8, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC", in Spanish "Cierra 8 ene 2026, 12:00 a.m. UTC". The poll page, the poll list and the embed keep the long date.
- The top line names the question count only where the card offers no answers (no ballot client), since otherwise the note above them already says it.
- Tests: the stream's stories check that every card's "Closes" line is one line box (failing on iteration 5's long date at the phone's 360px), that the top line no longer says "3 questions" where answers are offered, and that `NoBallotClient` still does.

Record: before, at 360 by 640 every card's closing time took two lines and the Riverside card's top line four, and the first card's heading began 939px down; after, each closing time is one line, the Riverside card's top line two ("Asked by Riverside Neighbours" over its closing time), and the first card's heading begins 919px down. In Spanish the time fits one line as well. At 1280 nothing moves but the shorter date.

### The stream and its card: iteration 7

The stream after iteration 6 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`, and as one following a card's topic chip and searching a place.

Review:

- **On a topic's page every card repeated the topic**: each card's "Where it's listed" line drew the page's own topic as a chip, under a title that already names it, so on `/discover?topic=transport` "Transport" read once in the title and once more on every card. A place's page did the same with the place, on every card, under the line "Showing questions filed at the place you entered". The stories hid this, since their cards weren't filed under the topic or place the story narrows by; as the API serves them, every card on such a page is.
- **The unreadable card's words sat 4px left of every other card's at desktop width**: the poll cards are padded 24px from `sm` up, the unreadable card 20px at every width, so its heading and note broke the column the headings above it make.
- Kept: the closing time on one line, the question count said once, the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold and its ring's room, the five-line lede, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, each count's two clean lines at 360px, times that keep their clock and "UTC" whole, the unreadable card in everyday words, counts per tier and never summed (T-01), the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), a civic-register time for an everyday question, shared with every surface that draws `When`.

Fix:

- `StreamCard` takes the page's narrowing (`narrowed`) and leaves out the topic when it is the page's topic and the place whenever the page is narrowed by a place (the place matches whole, ignoring capitals and spacing, so every card there is filed at it). The other tag still shows; a card left with neither shows no "Where it's listed" line. Home and the profile page narrow nothing, so their cards are unchanged.
- The unreadable card is padded as the poll cards are (`sm:p-6`).
- Tests: the `Topic` and `Place` stories now list cards filed under the topic or at the place, as the API would, and check that no card names it while the other tag still shows (both fail on iteration 6's card); the stream's stories check that the unreadable card's link starts where the readable cards' headings do (failing on iteration 6's padding at desktop width); the SSR test checks that a topic's page links its topic only from the orders and the topics' list.

Record: with cards filed as the page narrows, at 360 by 640 a card with no other tag (the bus card) is 52px shorter on a topic's page (348px to 296px), and on a place's page the Riverside and bus cards are 52px shorter each, the page 104px shorter in all (3,616px to 3,512px). At 1280 the unreadable card's heading starts 65px from the left, as every other card's does (61px before), and the card is 8px taller. Nothing else moves, at either width or in either theme.

### The stream and its card: iteration 8

The stream after iteration 7 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`, tapping each of a card's links in turn.

Review:

- **The asker's name was the smallest thing to tap on the page**: "Riverside Neighbours" in "Asked by Riverside Neighbours" was a link 14px tall, where each card's heading is 24px, its topic and place chips 32px and its answers 48px. Its line of small text allows it under WCAG 2.5.8's inline exception, but on a phone it is a link on its own above the question, and a thumb aimed at it lands on the closing time or the gap above the heading.
- **At 360px the note above a poll's answers wrapped "of 3." alone**: "These answers are for question 1" over "of 3.", splitting the one fact the note gives.
- Kept: the shared chip left off a narrowed page's cards, the unreadable card in the cards' column, the closing time on one line, the question count said once, the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold and its ring's room, the five-line lede, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, each count's two clean lines at 360px, times that keep their clock and "UTC" whole, counts per tier and never summed (T-01), the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), a civic-register time for an everyday question, shared with every surface that draws `When`.

Fix:

- `AskedBy` draws an invisible box 24px tall, centred on the name and as wide as it, that takes the tap; it reaches 5px past the line and moves nothing. The poll page's "Asked by" line, which uses the same component, gains it too.
- "1 of {count}" in the note and in the card's longer sentence for a page that doesn't explain once (`streamPickFirst`, `streamPick`) is joined by no-break spaces, in English and in Spanish ("1 de {count}").
- Tests: the stream's stories check that points 11px above and below the middle of the asker's name land on its link (failing on iteration 7's 14px link at both widths), and that "1 of 3" is one line box (failing on iteration 7's note at the phone's 360px); the SSR test expects the no-break spaces in the home page's note.

Record: at 360 by 640 the note reads "These answers are for" over "question 1 of 3.", still two lines, and the asker's name now answers a tap anywhere in a 24px band, where it took one only on its 14px of text. No card's height changes at 320, 360 or 1280 (516, 692, 296 and 198px at 360), and nothing else moves, in either theme.

### The stream and its card: iteration 9

The stream after iteration 8 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, and with phone verification on), read aloud card by card as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`, against [[language]] and the `everyday-stream` mockup.

Review:

- **Each card's count named its tier as a code**: "1,204 people · Tier 0 (verified e-mail)" and "1 person · Tier 2 (document-verified)". [[language]] says the tier leads in words ("among 2,418 people with an account"), never as a code ("Tier 0"), and the mockup's card says "people … have answered so far" in the same way. A first-time visitor doesn't know what Tier 0 or Tier 2 is, and the stream never explains it; the words in brackets were the only part that told them anything.
- **On a phone each count took two lines for one fact**: iteration 2 split the count and its tier onto lines of their own so the dot between them wouldn't end a line, so a poll with two tiers spent four lines of its "Answered so far" band on two numbers, between the question and its answers.
- Kept: the band before the answers, as the mockup has it; each tier on its own line and never one figure across them (T-01); Tier 0 named by what this deployment checks, so a card never claims a phone check the issuer doesn't make (T-01); the asker's 24px tap, "1 of 3" whole, the shared chip left off a narrowed page's cards, the unreadable card in the cards' column, the closing time on one line, the question count said once, the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), shared with every surface that draws `When`. The poll page, the poll list and the results still name tiers as "Tier 0 (verified e-mail)" in their facts and results, the civic register's evidence; they are their own surfaces' iterations.

Fix:

- Each count is one phrase with its tier in words: "1,204 people with a verified e-mail", "… with a verified e-mail and phone" where Tier 0 asks for a phone, "… vouched for by others" for Tier 1 and "… verified with a passport or ID" for Tier 2, with the singular for one, in English and in Spanish (`streamAnswered_*`, chosen by `answeredMessage` as `tierMessage` chooses the tier's name). The dot and the phone's two-line split are gone: the phrase wraps as a sentence.
- Tests: the stream's stories check each count's words, and that no card names a tier as "Tier n" (failing on iteration 8's card); `PhoneVerification` checks both cards' counts with the phone; the home page's story checks its card's count; the SSR test checks a topic's page's counts in words, including a tier with none at 0, and no "Tier n" on the card.

Record: at 360 by 640 the Riverside card's band (two tiers) is 112px tall instead of 132px, and the card 672px instead of 692px; with phone verification on, the bands are 88px and 132px instead of 108px and 152px. At 320 the Riverside band is 132px instead of 152px. At 1280 nothing moves: each count was one line there and still is. Both themes unchanged otherwise.

### The stream and its card: iteration 10

The stream after iteration 9 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, every state, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, and with phone verification on), read card by card as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`, measuring each count's lines at every width.

Review:

- **On a phone each count had little more than half the screen**: the "Answered so far" band sat inside the card's 20px padding with 16px of its own, so its words had 166px of a 320px screen and 206px of a 360px one, narrower than the question above them and the answers below. At 360 "1,204 people with a verified e-mail" wrapped "e-mail" alone onto a second line, and at 320 every count took two lines; with phone verification on, every count took two lines up to 390px.
- **Where a count did wrap, its last line was one word**, a short orphan `text-pretty` left at these lengths.
- Kept: each count's tier in words (iteration 9) and each tier on its own line, never one figure across them (T-01); the band before the answers, as the mockup has it; the asker's 24px tap, "1 of 3" whole, the shared chip left off a narrowed page's cards, the unreadable card in the cards' column, the question count said once, the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold, the orders on one row on a phone, the one explanation above the cards, the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), shared with every surface that draws `When`, and at 320 "Closes", the date and its clock take two lines. The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone, which every page shares, is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- Below 640px the band reaches the card's borders (`-mx-5`) with the card's 20px padding inside it, so its words start where the question's and the answers' do; from 640px it is inset as before.
- Each count wraps balanced (`text-balance`), so a two-line count splits into two even lines rather than leaving a word alone.
- Tests: the stream's stories check that the first card's count is one line box, and on a phone that it starts at the heading's left edge (both failing on iteration 9's band in the 360px phone project).

Record: the counts' text is 198px wide at 320 (166px before), 238px at 360 (206px) and 268px at 390 (236px). At 360 "1,204 people with a verified e-mail" and "38 people with a verified e-mail" are one line each, and the Library card's band is 20px shorter; at 390 every count without a phone is one line. Where a count still wraps (at 320, and with phone verification on) it splits as "1,204 people with / a verified e-mail". At 1280 nothing moves. Both themes unchanged otherwise.

The stream after iteration 10 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `PhoneVerification`, `Topic` and `Unreadable`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read top to bottom as a first-time visitor from a shared link to `/discover`, against [[language]] and the home page's own stream of cards.

Review:

- **The line above the cards promised answers no card had**: "Choose an answer on any card to open it in the ballot app. Nothing is sent until you cast your ballot there." was drawn over any page of cards where the site has a ballot client, so a page whose cards were all unreadable (the `Unreadable` state) told a visitor to choose an answer on cards that offer none, only a link to each poll's page. Home already says it only where a readable card is listed (`answerable`); the stream didn't.
- **An unreadable card's title left one word alone**: at 360 "This poll can't be shown here" wrapped "here" onto a line of its own (about 200px, then 38px), the orphan iteration 10 took from the counts.
- Kept: each count's tier in words, one line per tier, never summed (T-01); the band to the card's borders on a phone; the asker's 24px tap, "1 of 3" whole, the shared chip left off a narrowed page's cards, the unreadable card in the cards' column with its ID's start telling two apart, the question count said once, the cards at a reading width, the disclosure's fold, the orders on one row on a phone, the typed search and place only in their boxes (T-67), every order and topic a plain link. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), shared with every surface that draws `When`. The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- The line above the cards is drawn only when at least one card is a readable poll, as home's is.
- An unreadable card's title wraps balanced (`text-balance`).
- Tests: the `Unreadable` story checks that nothing above its cards says to choose an answer, and that each title's lines are balanced (no line under half the longest); both fail on iteration 10's stream in the 360px phone project.

### The stream and its card: iteration 13

The stream after iteration 12 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Place`, `PlaceRefused`, `Searching` and `Topic`, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640, 390 by 844 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read top to bottom as a visitor who tapped a card's place, then searched within it, measuring where the first card starts.

Review:

- **A place's page opened the whole fold for one box.** Since only the place's box says the place (T-67), a page narrowed by a place opened "Topic and place", and with it the fifteen topic links and their note, between the orders and the cards. On the `Place` state the first card started 1,686px down at 360 (858px on an unnarrowed page), 1,882px at 320 and 1,582px at 390. A topic's page already keeps the fold closed (iteration 3); a place's page put the topics back above its cards for a box that needs none of them. A refused place did the same, its box 1,270px down at 360 under the topics.
- Kept: the place never quoted outside its box (T-67), each narrowing dropped alone by its own 24px link 12px from the next line, the search's box first, the orders on one row on a phone, the fold closed on every other page; and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), shared with every surface that draws `When`. The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration. A place's page still puts two boxes, two hints and two "Clear" lines above its cards.

Fix:

- On a place's page, and on a refused place's, the place's box stands on its own after the search's, above the orders, and the fold stays closed with only the topics in it, its line "Topics" (the topics' own name, `discoverTopics`: a new message put `/verify`'s script 4 bytes over its budget). Every other page is unchanged.
- Tests: the `Place` story checks that the place's box is visible, that the fold is closed with "Topics" as its line, and that the box follows the search's and precedes the orders, the only place box on the page; `PlaceRefused` checks its box visible with the fold closed. Both fail on iteration 12's stream, whose fold is open.

Record: on the `Place` state the first card starts at 1,194px at 360 by 640 (1,686px before), 1,334px at 320 (1,882px), 1,166px at 390 (1,582px) and 963px at 1280 (1,115px); the place's box at 600px at 360 (1,270px). The refused place's page is 1,240px tall at 360 (1,732px), its box at 600px. Unnarrowed and topic pages are unchanged. Both themes unchanged otherwise.

### The stream and its card: iteration 14

The stream after iteration 13 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Place`, `PlaceRefused`, `Searching` and `Topic`, at 320 by 568, 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read top to bottom as a visitor who searched within a place, iteration 13's open finding.

Review:

- **Each box kept telling a visitor what to type after they had typed it.** Under the search, "Every word you type must appear in a question or its title, up to 8 words. Results keep the order below." (three lines at 360); under the place, "A town, neighbourhood or school, as askers write it. …" (three more). Neither said what the page now held.
- **What the page held, and the way to drop it, sat apart from the box it came from**: "Only the questions that hold every word you searched for." with "Clear the search" came after the place's box, the orders and the topics' fold, four blocks below the search box, so which box each "Clear" emptied showed only by its words. On the `Place` state the first card started at 1,194px at 360.
- Kept: the search and the place never quoted outside their boxes (T-67), each narrowing dropped alone by its own 24px link, the place's box beside the search's with the topics folded, a topic's "Show every topic" under the fold, the orders on one row on a phone; and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later, unchanged: each closing time in the stories reads as a midnight in UTC ("12:00 AM UTC"), shared with every surface that draws `When`. The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- A box that narrows the page says so under it, in our words, with its own "Clear" link, in place of its hint; that line describes the box (`aria-describedby`). A box that narrows nothing, or holds a refused search or place, keeps its hint. A topic's way back stays under the fold.
- Tests: the `Place` story checks that each "Clear" link is inside its own box's form, that neither hint is drawn, and that each box is described by its note; all fail on iteration 13's stream in both projects.

Record: on the `Place` state the first card starts at 1,038px at 360 by 640 (1,194px before), 1,158px at 320 (1,334px) and 867px at 1280 (963px), and "Clear the search" sits directly under the search box. Unnarrowed and topic pages are unchanged. Both themes unchanged otherwise.

Review:

- **The empty box asked for one thing and did another.** A page narrowed by a topic and a place with nothing in it said "Only polls whose askers chose this topic or place are listed. Try without one of them.", and its one button, "Show everything", dropped both and the topic's titled page with them. ### The stream and its card: iteration 19

When someone asks one, it shows up here.", with nothing to tap, to a visitor who had just read ten open questions; narrowed by a topic or a place, it drew their empty boxes instead, saying only questions filed there are listed and offering to drop a narrowing that had matched the page before. The only way back to the first page was the chosen order's link above.

On a later page the chosen order's link, which leads to the first page, is still marked as the page itself (`aria-current="page"`).

("Esas eran todas", "Todas las preguntas abiertas en este orden estaban en las páginas anteriores."), with "Start from the top" keeping the order, the search, the topic and the place. ### The stream and its card: iteration 22

The stream after iteration 21 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx` and `when.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `LaterPage`, `Topic`, `Place`, `Searching` and the new `UtcVisitor` and `SpanishClock`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor deciding whether they still have time to answer, the leftover every iteration since the 11th named.

Review:

- **Each card's closing time was in UTC, which can name the wrong day.** The card read "Closes Jan 8, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC" for a poll that, for a visitor in New York, closes on the evening of January 7. The poll form takes the asker's closing time on their own clock ("Eastern Time"), so an evening close anywhere in the Americas showed on the card as the next day's date, and a visitor reading it in the morning thought they had a day longer than they did. [[language]] keeps times in UTC for the evidence face; the everyday card is not evidence, and its only time is the one a visitor acts on.
- Kept: the abbreviated month and the clock with its zone kept whole on one line at 360 (iterations 3 and 6); the card's top line with who asked; a later page's note (iteration 21); and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC, so a visitor who opens a card's poll reads its close in UTC there; they are the poll page's and sharing's own iterations. The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- `When` takes `local`: once the page runs in the browser it draws the time on the visitor's own clock with that zone's short name as their language says it ("Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST", "8 ene 2026, 1:00 a.m. GMT+1"), the clock and zone kept whole as "UTC" was. The served page, and a visitor without scripts, keep UTC: the server never learns the visitor's zone, and the browser reads it only to draw the words (`useSyncExternalStore`, whose server value is none, so hydration matches the served text before redrawing). The `datetime` attribute keeps the instant in UTC. The stream's card (and so home's three) passes `local`; nothing else does yet.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark`, on New York's clock, check "Closes Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST" and the `datetime` unchanged; both fail on iteration 21's card. `UtcVisitor` checks a visitor on UTC still reads "12:00 AM UTC", and `SpanishClock` the Spanish words east of UTC. `tests/ssr.test.ts` checks the served stream's closing time is in UTC.

Record: on the `Default` story at 360 the first card's top line reads "Closes Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST" on one line where it read "Closes Jan 8, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC"; in Spanish east of UTC, "Cierra 8 ene 2026, 1:00 a.m. GMT+1", also one line. Page heights are unchanged (2,704px at 360). Both themes otherwise unchanged.

### The stream and its card: iteration 23

The stream after iteration 22 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`, `when.tsx` and `format.ts`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `LaterPage`, `Topic`, `Place`, `Searching` and the new `ClosesToday`, `ClosesTomorrow` and `SpanishTomorrow`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor in the "Closing soon" order deciding which question to answer before it closes.

Review:

- **A close later today read as a date the visitor had to recognise.** On the morning of January 7 in New York the first card read "Closes Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST". The order the visitor chose is about how soon, and the one fact it turns on, that the poll closes tonight, showed only to someone who knew today's date. A close the next day read the same way.
- Kept: the visitor's own clock and zone once the page runs, with UTC served (iteration 22); the clock and zone kept whole on one line at 360 (iterations 3 and 6); a later page's note (iteration 21); and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- `When`'s `local` time says the day as "today" or "tomorrow" ("hoy", "mañana", from the browser's `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`) when the instant falls on one of them on the visitor's calendar, read in their zone; any other day keeps its date ("Closes today, 7:00 PM EST", "Closes Jan 14, 2026, 7:00 PM EST"). The day is judged only in the browser, never on the server, which knows neither the visitor's zone nor when a cached page is read; the served page keeps UTC and its date. The `datetime` attribute keeps the instant in UTC. A story fixes the clock through a `ViewerNow` context, as `ViewerTimeZone` fixes the zone.
- Tests: `ClosesToday` checks "Closes today, 7:00 PM EST" on one line at either width, the week-later card's date unchanged and `datetime` unchanged; `ClosesTomorrow` checks "tomorrow" on the visitor's calendar where in UTC the close is two days on; `SpanishTomorrow` checks "Cierra mañana, 1:00 a.m. GMT+1" east of UTC. Each fails on iteration 22's `When`. The stories' default clock is New Year's Day, a week before the first close, so every earlier story reads as before. Script budgets go up by 1 KiB for home (181), the stream (178), the poll page (218) and the poll form (255), the last two for the catalog's one new line.

Record: on `ClosesToday` at 360 the first card's top line reads "Closes today, 7:00 PM EST" on one line where it read "Closes Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST"; the Riverside card, a week on, still reads "Closes Jan 14, 2026, 7:00 PM EST". Page heights are unchanged (2,704px at 360). Both themes otherwise unchanged.

### The stream and its card: iteration 24

The stream after iteration 23 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`, `when.tsx` and `format.ts`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `Searching`, `LaterPage`, `ClosesToday` and the new `OpenedToday`, `OpenedLastWeek`, `OpenedLastYear` and `SpanishOpened`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor in the "New" order weighing which questions are fresh and how many have answered.

Review:

- **A card's count said nothing of how long it had gathered.** "Answered so far: 38 people with a verified e-mail" read the same for a poll asked this morning and for one open two weeks, so a visitor couldn't tell a question taking off from one nobody answers. And under "New", the order about how recently a poll was asked ("Open now, the most recently asked first."), no card said when: iteration 23 gave "Closing soon" the fact its order turns on, and "New" still had none.
- Kept: the closing time on the visitor's own clock with "today" and "tomorrow" (iterations 22 and 23); each tier's count on its own line, never summed (T-01); the band reaching the card's borders on a phone (iteration 10); a later page's note (iteration 21); and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- The count's heading says since when: "Answered since it opened today", "… yesterday", "… Jan 1", with the year only when it isn't this year ("Dec 31, 2025"); in Spanish "Han respondido desde que abrió hoy", "… el 1 ene". The day is the poll's opening on the visitor's own calendar, read only once the page runs (`useViewerDaySince` in `when.tsx`, as iteration 22's zone): the served page, and a visitor without scripts, keep "Answered so far", since the server knows neither the visitor's zone nor when a cached page is read, and a day in UTC can be the wrong one. A date's spaces are no-break ones, so at 360px "el 1 ene" wraps whole where "ene" fell alone onto a second line. The list of counts is named by its heading, so a screen reader hears it too. The top line gains nothing: at 360 it already holds who asked and the close.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` check "Answered since it opened yesterday" (midnight UTC is New Year's Eve in New York); `OpenedToday` checks "today" and the heading on one line at 360; `OpenedLastWeek` checks "Jan 1" on UTC's calendar; `OpenedLastYear` the year; `SpanishOpened` the Spanish words and "el 1 ene" on one line. Each fails on iteration 23's card. Home's story checks its count under the new heading. `tests/ssr.test.ts` still checks the served stream says "Answered so far". Script budgets unchanged.

Record: on `Default` at 360 the Riverside card's grey band reads "Answered since it opened yesterday" on one line where it read "Answered so far", above the same two counts; in Spanish east of UTC two days on, "Han respondido desde que abrió" over "el 1 ene". Page heights are unchanged (2,704px at 360). Both themes otherwise unchanged.

### The stream and its card: iteration 25

The stream after iteration 24 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `MostAnswered`, `LaterPage`, `Topic`, `Place`, `Empty`, `NoBallotClient`, `PhoneVerification` and the new `NoAnswersYet` and `SpanishNoAnswersYet`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor under "New", where the top of the order is the questions asked most recently and so, most of the time, ones nobody has answered yet.

Review:

- **A poll nobody had answered read as a band of zeros.** The count is drawn tier by tier (T-01), and a tier with no voter is "0", so a question asked this morning read "Answered since it opened today", then "0 people with a verified e-mail", and on a poll open to two tiers "0 people verified with a passport or ID" under it, wrapped onto a fourth line at 360. Two lines of noughts in the grey band looked like a count that had failed to load rather than a question waiting for its first answer, and they took the most room on exactly the cards "New" puts first.
- Kept: each tier's count on its own line, never summed, wherever anyone has answered, a zero tier's line included beside a counted one (T-01); the heading saying since when on the visitor's own calendar (iteration 24); the closing time on the visitor's own clock with "today" and "tomorrow" (iterations 22 and 23); the band reaching the card's borders on a phone (iteration 10); a later page's note (iteration 21); and everything iteration 11 kept on the cards. No width scrolls sideways.
- Still for later: a card whose count couldn't be read draws no band, as one on a profile's page draws none, so it reads the same as a poll whose count isn't shown at all; the read fails only when the API does, and the poll page's own count is the poll page's iteration. Under "Most answered this week" the cards are ordered by the last seven days' count while each card shows its count since it opened, so a long-open poll with more answers in all can sit below a newer one; the listing serves no week's count per poll, so saying it on the card waits on the API. The poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- Where every tier's count is zero, the band says it once: "No one yet" ("Nadie todavía") under its heading, in place of a line per tier. That is one fact about every tier, true of each, so it sums nothing (T-01). As soon as any tier has an answer the band lists every tier as before, its zeros too, so a count never hides a tier beside a counted one.
- Tests: `NoAnswersYet` checks that the two unanswered cards read "Answered since it opened today" and "No one yet", with no "0 …" line and no list of counts, and that a card answered in one tier still lists both, "3 people with a verified e-mail" and "0 people verified with a passport or ID"; `SpanishNoAnswersYet` checks "Nadie todavía" on both. Both fail on iteration 24's card. Every earlier story is unchanged. Script budgets unchanged.

Record: on `NoAnswersYet` at 360 the Riverside card's grey band reads "Answered since it opened today" over "No one yet", two lines where it read four ("0 people with a verified e-mail", then "0 people verified" and "with a passport or ID"); the library card's band reads the same two lines where it read "0 people with a verified e-mail". Every other story's screenshots match iteration 24's. Both themes otherwise unchanged.

### The stream and its card: iteration 27

The stream after iteration 26 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default` and `Dark`, and home's and a profile's cards, which are the same component, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read on a phone as a visitor who wants to know more about a question before answering it.

Review:

- **A card's only way to its poll's page looked like plain text.** Each card's heading links to the poll's own page (its full wording, how it is counted, when it opened and closes, its sharing), and the answers below lead out to the ballot client. The heading was underlined only under a mouse's hover, so on a phone, which has none, it read as a title: the visible links on the card were the asker's name, the topic and place chips and the answers, and a visitor who wanted the details before choosing had nothing to tell them the heading opens them. The unreadable card's link beside it was underlined, so of two links to a poll's page on one screen only one looked like one.
- Kept: the heading's size and weight; the answers as full-width boxes; the count's heading saying since when (iteration 24) and "No one yet" (iteration 25); the notes' reading width (iteration 26). No width scrolls sideways, and page heights are unchanged (2,704px at 360).
- Still for later: a card whose count couldn't be read draws no band (iteration 25). The poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- The heading's link is underlined at rest, thin and in the brand's colour (`decoration-primary decoration-1 underline-offset-4`), thickening under a mouse, so it reads as a link without outweighing the words. Home's and a profile's cards get it too, being the same card.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` check that the heading's link is underlined with no hover. Both fail on iteration 26's card. Every other story is unchanged. Script budgets unchanged.

Record: on `Default` at 360 "Library hours" carries a thin underline 4px below it, where it read as unmarked text; in dark the underline is the dark theme's primary. Every other screenshot matches iteration 26's. Both themes otherwise unchanged.

### The stream and its card: iteration 28

The stream after iteration 27 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx` and `format.ts`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `SpanishClock` and the closing stories, and home's and a profile's cards, which are the same component, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor scanning the cards' top lines to decide which question to answer before it closes.

Review:

- **Each card's close said a year the visitor is living in, and its count beside it didn't.** On the visitor's own clock a close beyond tomorrow read "Closes Jan 14, 2026, 7:00 PM EST", while the count under the same card's question read "Answered since it opened Jan 1", which leaves out the current year (iteration 24). A card thus dated its two days two ways, and the close's line, the first thing on a card without an asker, spent "2026, " on the one fact no visitor needs: at 360 it was 31 characters of a 34-character line.
- Kept: "today" and "tomorrow" for a close near the visitor's day (iteration 23); the visitor's zone named after the clock; the served page's UTC, which says its year as before, being the page without the visitor's calendar; the line's one row at every width. No width scrolls sideways, and page heights are unchanged (2,704px at 360, 2,417px at 1280).
- Still for later: a card whose count couldn't be read draws no band (iteration 25). The poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- `formatZonedNear` draws a close in `now`'s year (on the visitor's calendar) as month, day and time without the year ("Jan 14, 7:00 PM", "14 ene, 7:00 p.m."), its last space a no-break one as before; a close in another year keeps it. `formatZonedDaySince` shares the same year test.
- Tests: `Default`, `Dark`, `UtcVisitor`, `SpanishClock`, `ClosesToday` and `ClosesTomorrow` expect the close without this year, each failing on iteration 27's card; the new `ClosesNextYear` story keeps "Jan 14, 2027" on a close in the next year beside "Jan 7" on one in this. The stream, home and profile Storybook projects pass. Script budgets unchanged.

Record: on `Default` at 360 the library's top line reads "Closes Jan 7, 7:00 PM EST" where it read "Closes Jan 7, 2026, 7:00 PM EST", matching its count's "since it opened yesterday" style; in `SpanishClock`, "Cierra 8 ene, 1:00 a.m. GMT+1". Every other screenshot matches iteration 27's, in both themes.
