### The stream and its card: iteration 31

The stream after iteration 30 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark` and `SpanishClock`, and home's and a profile's cards, which are the same component, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor going down the column comparing one card with the next.

Review:

- **A card whose count couldn't be read said nothing.** Since iteration 25 a card whose `GET /elections/answered` failed drew no band at all, listed under "still for later" since. On `Default` the bus card sat between two cards each with a grey band of counts, and went straight from its question to its answers: a visitor comparing cards read it as a poll that isn't counted, or one nobody may answer, when only our server's read had failed. `undefined` meant both "this page shows no count" (a profile's own cards) and "the read failed", so the card couldn't tell them apart.
- Kept: the other cards' bands, "No one yet" (iteration 25) and every count's tiers on their own lines (T-01); the cards' and answers' widths (iterations 29 and 30); a profile's own cards, which show no count, unchanged. Page heights grow by the band: 2,704px to 2,788px at 360, 2,417px to 2,481px at 1280.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration.

Fix:

- A stream card's `answered` is `"unread"` when the stream's or home's loader (`loadCard`) couldn't read the count, apart from `undefined` where a page shows none. The card draws the same band, in muted text, saying "We couldn't get how many have answered just now." ("Ahora mismo no pudimos saber cuántos han respondido."): no figure, not even nobody (T-01). Home's featured question still needs a read count with someone in it.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` check that the bus card says so, claims no count, and that its band starts and ends where the library card's count does, above the answers; `SpanishClock` checks the Spanish. The SSR stream test checks the served card says it. Both fail on iteration 30's card. Script budgets unchanged: `/account`'s two new catalog lines fit under the 201 KiB that [[P2-19]] part 48 (#995) set.

Record: on `Default` at 360 and 1280, light and dark, the bus card now has a grey band under its question, as the two above it do, with the one muted line where their counts are; at 360 it wraps to two lines. Every other card matches iteration 30's.

### The stream and its card: iteration 32

The stream after iteration 31 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `MostAnswered`, `LaterPage`, `Topic`, `Place`, `Searching`, `NoAnswersYet`, `NoBallotClient` and `PhoneVerification`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor who chose "Most answered this week" and compares the figures down the column.

Review:

- **The order and the cards counted different things, and nothing said so.** "Most answered this week" ranks the open polls by the people who answered in the last seven days' whole anchoring periods, every tier together, while each card's band counts every answer since the poll opened, tier by tier (T-01). A poll open for a month with 900 answers, 20 of them this week, sits below one opened yesterday with 40, so a visitor reading the bands down the page sees 40 above 900 under an order named for the most answered, and takes it for an order gone wrong. Iteration 25 noted it as waiting on the API serving a week's count per poll; iterations 26 to 31 dropped it from "still for later" without fixing it. The card can't show the order's own figure, which adds the tiers together (T-01), so the page says how the two differ.
- Kept: the order's note under the orders, at the search box's width (iteration 26), and none under an order that couldn't be drawn (iteration 18); each card's band since the poll opened on the visitor's calendar (iteration 24), "No one yet" (iteration 25) and the unread band (iteration 31); the cards' and answers' widths (iterations 29 and 30). The asker's 24px tap (iteration 8), the chips' 32px and the answers' 48px hold at both widths. No width scrolls sideways; page heights are unchanged (`Default` 2,788px at 360 and 2,481px at 1280; `MostAnswered` 3,532px and 2,825px), as the note stays five lines at 360 and two at 1280.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration. A card's own count of the order's seven days, tier by tier, would let the bands follow the order; the API serves none.

Fix:

- The order's note says it: "Open now, the most answered in the last seven days first, each person counted once. A card counts every answer since it opened, so it can show more than the one above it." ("Abiertas ahora, las más respondidas en los últimos siete días primero, cada persona contada una vez. Cada tarjeta cuenta todas las respuestas desde que abrió, así que puede mostrar más que la de arriba."). It gives up "Anyone can count it again from the public board", which home's "Counted again by anyone" and the closed poll's page say where a visitor checks a count, and the re-vote's detail, kept as "each person counted once", to stay five lines on a phone.
- Tests: `MostAnswered` checks the note's sentence on the cards' count; it fails on iteration 31's note. The stream Storybook projects and the SSR and stream tests pass. Script budgets unchanged.

Record: on `MostAnswered` at 360 the note under the orders reads five lines ending "so it can show more than the one above it.", where it ended "Anyone can count it again from the public board."; at 1280 two lines within the search box's width. Every card and every other story matches iteration 31's, in both themes.

### The stream and its card: iteration 33

The stream after iteration 32 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default` and `Dark`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a keyboard user tabbing from the search box down the column to "More questions".

Review:

- **A card's text links drew a second focus style.** Every box on the page (the orders, the fold, the chips, the answers, "More questions") draws the system's focus: a square 3px ring in the ring's colour. The links drawn as underlined text drew none of their own, so they took the browser's: a thin, rounded outline 1px out. On a card's heading at 1280 it ran through the heading's own underline and the descenders of "Library hours"; on the asker's name it sat on the underline; on the unreadable card's title and the lines under the boxes ("Clear", "Show every topic", "Start from the top") the same. Going down one card a keyboard user met the rounded outline on the heading, the square ring on the chips and answers, then the rounded one again on the next card's asker, two looks for one thing on a design whose edges are all square.
- Kept: the boxes' rings, unchanged; the heading's underline at rest (iteration 27) and the asker's 24px tap (iteration 8); the lines under the boxes and their 24px taps (iterations 12 and 15). Nothing moves without focus: page heights are unchanged (`Default` 2,788px at 360 and 2,481px at 1280).
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration. A card's own count of the order's seven days, tier by tier, would let the bands follow the order; the API serves none. Text links elsewhere on the site (home's, a profile's, the poll page's) still take the browser's outline: each surface's own iteration.

Fix:

- `focus-text`, a utility in `apps/web/src/styles.css`: a solid 3px outline in the ring's colour, 2px out, on focus seen from the keyboard. The stream's card heading, the unreadable card's title, the asker's name (`AskedBy`, so home's and a profile's cards too), the boxes' "Clear" lines, "Show every topic" and "Start from the top" take it. An outline rather than the boxes' ring (a box shadow): a heading wrapped onto two or three lines at 360 gets one square shape round all of them, clear of each line's underline, where a ring drew a box per line over the underline of the line above. A utility, not the three Tailwind classes on each link: written into the markup they took the poll page's script 495 bytes past its 218 KiB budget, and `/` and `/discover` past theirs once the asker's copy was split out.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` focus the library's heading, the asker and the unreadable card's title, check each is focus-visible and draws a solid 3px outline 2px out. Both fail on iteration 32's card (the browser's `auto 1px`). The stream, home and profile Storybook projects pass, and every page stays within its script budget, unchanged.

Record: on `Default` at 1280, the library's heading focused draws a square green frame 2px clear of its letters and its underline, matching the chips' and answers' frames below it, where a thin rounded line crossed the underline; the asker's name likewise. At 360 the bus card's three-line heading focused draws one stepped frame round the three lines, every underline visible. Unfocused, every screenshot matches iteration 32's, in both themes.

### The stream and its card: iteration 35

The stream after iteration 34 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; stories `Components/Stream`, `Default`, `Dark`, `MostAnswered`, `Topic` and `Unreadable`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read on a phone as a visitor who reads a card's band to see who has answered.

Review:

- **A tier's line that wrapped read as two tiers.** The band lists one tier per line, 4px apart, and on a 360px screen in the site's frame the Riverside card's second tier wrapped: "1 person verified" on one line and "with a passport or ID" flush under it, at the same edge and nearly the same spacing as the rows above. Read down the band it was three rows, the last a count with no number, and a visitor counting tiers saw one more than the poll has. Every tier's words are long enough to wrap somewhere (the phone tier's, Spanish's, a 320px screen or 125% zoom), so the band needs a way to tell a row's second line from the next row.
- Kept: one line per tier and never a figure across them (T-01); the tiers named in words, never as a code; the band's title saying since when (iteration 24), "No one yet" (iteration 25) and the unread band (iteration 31); the band reaching the card's borders on a phone (iteration 10), so a one-tier count like the library's keeps one line at 360. The orders' two-line labels at 360 were chosen in iteration 4 and stay. No width scrolls sideways; page heights are unchanged.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's 40px side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration. A card's own count of the order's seven days, tier by tier, would let the bands follow the order; the API serves none. Text links elsewhere on the site (home's, a profile's, the poll page's) still take the browser's outline: each surface's own iteration.

Fix:

- Each tier's line hangs its continuation 16px in (`pl-4 -indent-4`), as a list's wrapped item does: every row's first line starts at the band's edge, where it did, and a wrapped row's later lines sit under its words, not under the next row's start. Home's and a profile's cards share the band and take it too.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` check each tier's row hangs 16px, its first line starts where the other rows' do, and any later line starts 16px in; they fail on iteration 34's band (no indent). The stream, home and profile Storybook projects, the SSR tests (with the script budgets, unchanged) and the stream tests pass.

Record: on `Default` at 360 in the frame, the Riverside band's passport tier wraps with its second line 16px in from the rows above, so the band reads as two tiers; before, the second line sat flush and the band read as three. At 1280, and on every card whose tiers fit one line, every screenshot matches iteration 34's, in both themes.

### The stream and its card: iteration 36

The stream after iteration 35 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; every story under `Components/Stream`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, then each at the narrow widths the preview names: a folded phone's 280px and a 360px phone zoomed to 125%, 288px), read as a visitor on a small or zoomed screen who meets each of the stream's dead ends.

At 360 in Storybook's padding the column already ran 8px into the gutter. Every other story stays within its column at those widths; this was the only one.

The stream Storybook projects and the SSR tests (with the script budgets, unchanged) pass.

### The stream and its card: iteration 37

The stream after iteration 36 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; every story under `Components/Stream`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, then each at a folded phone's 280px, a 360px phone zoomed to 125% (288px) and 320px), read as a Spanish-speaking visitor on a folded phone.

Review:

- **The orders' row was wider than a folded phone's column in Spanish.** Below `sm` the three orders share one row, each label wrapping in its own box (an earlier iteration: wrapped as whole links, the third took a row alone at 360). In Spanish the row's longest words, "Nuevas", "Cierran" and "respondidas", need 243px with the boxes' padding and gaps. Storybook's check that no page scrolls sideways failed on every Spanish story at 280 (by 3px). On the site, with its 16px gutter, the column there is 248px, so the row fit with 5px to spare: a slightly larger text setting scrolled the page sideways. In English the row fits.
- Kept: one row at 360 and wider, in both languages, with the longest label narrowing most; the orders as plain links; their 48px boxes.
- Still for later: the poll page's facts, its summary and the embed still say UTC (the poll page's and sharing's own iterations). The frame's side gutter on a phone is the frame's own iteration. A card's own count of the order's seven days, tier by tier, would let the bands follow the order; the API serves none. Text links elsewhere on the site (home's, a profile's, the poll page's) still take the browser's outline: each surface's own iteration.

Fix:

- The orders' `nav` is a size container (`@container`), and in a column under 17rem (272px) the row wraps by whole links (`@max-[17rem]:flex-wrap`). Each link keeps `flex-auto`, so "Nuevas" and "Cierran pronto" share the first row and "Más respondidas esta semana" fills the second. The column's width rather than the screen's: the frame's gutter decides what is left. Letting the links shrink below their words (`min-w-0`) was tried and dropped: it squeezed "Nuevas" past its own box rather than wrapping the longest label.
- Tests: `SpanishOrdersFolded` (es-US, the folded viewport) checks the page doesn't scroll sideways, the first two orders share a row and the third sits below them, every link ends inside the list, and each label is one line. It fails on iteration 36's row in both Storybook projects. The stream Storybook projects and the website's tests (with the script budgets, unchanged) pass.

At 360 and 1280, in both languages, every screenshot matches iteration 36's, in both themes.

The stream Storybook projects and the website's tests pass.

Record: on `Place` at 1280 both fields now end at 616px and both buttons span 624px to 712px; at 360 both fields end at 224px and both buttons span 232px to 320px, where the place's had been 17px longer. Elsewhere the only change is the search button, 1px wider in English and 2px in Spanish ("Buscar", 86px before), with its field narrower by as much.

### The stream and its card: iteration 39

The stream after iteration 38 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; every story under `Components/Stream`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark), read as a visitor on a topic's page who opens "Topic and place" to choose another topic.

Review:

- **The topic's way back stayed on the page when the fold opened, under the place's box.** A topic's page keeps the topics folded and offers "Show every topic" under the fold's line, so the way back needs no fifteen topics. Opened, the fold puts the topics first, led by "All topics", which goes to the same address, and then the place's box; "Show every topic" stayed after it all, 24px under the place's note. So the opened fold offered the same way out twice, and the second sat where the place's own "Clear the place" sits on a place's page, reading as the place's. It also held the first card 48px further down (1,476px at 360, 1,127px at 1280).
- Kept: the fold closed on a topic's page, with "Show every topic" under its line; "All topics" first among the topics, marked current on the unnarrowed stream; the place's box and note in the fold; both themes passing axe.
- Still for later: as iteration 37 listed (the poll page's, sharing's and the frame's own iterations; a card's own seven-day count, which the API doesn't serve; other surfaces' text links).

Fix:

- The fold is a Tailwind `peer`, and "Show every topic", its next sibling, is `peer-open:hidden`: hidden while the fold is open, by CSS, so it holds before the page's script loads, and back when the fold closes.
- Tests: `Topic` checks that the link is gone once the fold opens and visible again once it closes. It fails on iteration 38's page in both Storybook projects. The stream Storybook projects (76 passed) and the website's tests pass.

Record: on `Topic` with the fold open, the place's note is now the fold's last line, and the first card starts at 1,428px at 360 (1,476px before) and 1,079px at 1280 (1,127px). With the fold closed, and on every other story, every screenshot matches iteration 38's, in both themes.

### The stream and its card: iteration 40

The stream after iteration 39 (`apps/web/src/components/stream.tsx`; every story under `Components/Stream`, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, with every link's target and size measured), read as a visitor on "Most answered this week" who taps a card's topic to see more like it.

Review:

- **A card's topic and place forgot the page they were on.** Each card's "Where it's listed" chips led to the topic's or place's newest (`/discover?topic=culture`), whatever the page's order, search or other narrowing. The fold's topics above the cards, the same words, keep all three, and the place's box keeps the order and the search. So on "Most answered this week" two links named "Culture" on one page led to two pages: the fold's to Culture's most answered, the card's to Culture's newest, with "New" now marked current and nothing said about the change. On a search, the card's "Eastside" dropped the words that had found the card, so the page it opened might not hold it.
- Kept: the chips' look and 32px height (WCAG 2.5.8 is 24px; they sit in their own row, 8px apart); the asker's link with its 24px tap box (iteration 8); a chip left out where the page already narrows by it (iteration 7); the poll page's and the register's chips leading to the newest, as neither has an order; both themes passing axe.
- Still for later: as iteration 37 listed (the poll page's, sharing's and the frame's own iterations; a card's own seven-day count, which the API doesn't serve; other surfaces' text links).

Fix:

- `ListedUnder` takes `within`, the stream page's order and narrowing, and each link keeps them with its own topic or place in place of the page's; `StreamCard` passes it when given the stream's `sort`, which only the stream page does. Home, a profile's page, the poll page and the register keep their links to the newest. Kept, a chip leads to a page that still holds its card, as a fold topic's does.
- Tests: `MostAnswered` checks that a card's "Culture" leads where the fold's "Culture" does (`/discover?sort=answered&topic=culture`) and its "Eastside" keeps the order; `Searching` that "Eastside" keeps the order and the search; `Place` that the card's "Culture" keeps the place and the search. All three fail on iteration 39's cards in both Storybook projects. The stream, home, poll summary, register and profile Storybook projects (192 passed) and the website's tests (1,125 passed) pass.

Record: no screenshot changes, in either theme at either width; only the chips' targets do. On `MostAnswered` the first card's "Culture" now leads to `/discover?sort=answered&topic=culture`, where it led to `/discover?topic=culture`.

### The poll page: iteration 1

The poll page in the states that show its words (`apps/web/src/components/poll-summary.tsx`; stories `Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedDark`, `WordedQuestions`, `WordedRightToLeft`, `WordedScheduled`, `WordedNoBallot`, `Closed` and `InShort`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, read as a visitor who taps a friend's link to a one-question poll, and as one who arrives from a stream card.

Review:

- **The answers looked tappable and weren't.** "What it asks" drew each answer in a bordered box, the box a stream card draws its answers in, where tapping one opens the ballot app with it chosen ([[P2-23]] part 2b), as the embed's answers do too. On the poll page they were list items: a visitor who came from a card, or who simply tapped "Yes", got nothing, and had to find "Answer the question" further down. The question is the hero and answering takes a tap or two ([[PROJECT_BRIEF]], Design direction), and here the answers were the one thing on the page that looked like a control and did nothing.
- **The one way in was two screens down.** On a 360 by 640 phone "Answer the question" started at 1,256px (1,707px on the two-question poll), after the lede, the four facts and the question's own box; at 1280 by 800 at 881px, under the first screen too.
- Kept: the lede saying the answer can be changed until the close; the facts (who can vote, opens, closes, counting) before the questions; "Answer the question" in the Answering box with its note, for a visitor who wants the ballot app without choosing first; the scheduled, closed and no-ballot states taking no answers; both themes.
- Still for later: the facts still hold the first tap below a small phone's first screen (the first answer starts at 991px at 360); the facts and the summary still say UTC (from the stream's iterations); the page's text links (the moderation note's, the evidence link) still take the browser's outline; the answered count, reactions and follow sit as three blocks between the answers and the evidence.

Fix:

- On an open poll this page links answering for (its words shown, a ballot client named), the first question's answers are links into the ballot client with `&pick=<option>` in the fragment, drawn as a stream card's are (48px tall, full width, an arrow), under the card's own words ("Choose an answer to open it in the ballot app. Nothing is sent until you cast your ballot there.", or "to question 1 of N" on a poll of several). A link carries one pick, for question 1, as the card's and the embed's do, so later questions' answers stay words. The list is named by its question, or by the page's heading where the one question heads the page. The pick, like the poll, is in the fragment alone, which no server sees (T-10, T-11).
- Answers that aren't links (later questions, and every question of a scheduled, closed or hidden poll, or one on a deployment with no ballot client) are drawn on the muted fill with no border, so nothing that can't be tapped wears the box that can.
- Tests: `Worded` and `WordedDark` check each answer's link and pick, its 48px height and the card's words; `WordedQuestions` that question 1's answers are links named by their question and question 2's are borderless words; `WordedScheduled` that no answer is a link or boxed; `WordedNoBallot` and `InShort` that none is a link. The four open and scheduled stories fail on the page before this iteration in both Storybook projects (8 failed). The poll summary Storybook projects (74 passed) and the website's tests (1,130 passed, with the served page's picks for both open polls) pass.

Record: at 360 by 640 the first answer now starts at 991px (1,068px on the two-question poll), where the first tap was "Answer the question" at 1,256px (1,707px); at 1280 by 800 at 658px, inside the first screen, where it was 881px. The links make the page 184px taller at 360 (3,388px) and 162px at 1280 (2,531px), and "Answer the question" sits that much lower. The scheduled, closed and hidden states change only in their answers' boxes, now filled rather than bordered.

### The poll page: iteration 2

The same stories as iteration 1 (`Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedDark`, `WordedQuestions`, `WordedScheduled`, `WordedNoBallot`, `Closed` and `InShort`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, read as a visitor who taps a friend's link on a phone, against the open poll's mockup (`everyday-poll-page.tsx`).

Review:

- **The facts still held the first answer under a phone's first screen** (iteration 1's first item left for later). The four facts stack one to a row below 640px, 358px tall, between the lede and "What it asks", so at 360 by 640 the first answer started at 991px. The open poll's mockup has no fact strip before the questions: its hero is the byline, the title, the lede and then the questions, with answering beside them.
- **The one question that heads the page was numbered "1"** in a 2.5rem column, beside nothing: its words are the page's heading, so its card holds only the instruction and the answers, and the column took a fifth of a 360px phone's card from them.
- Tried and dropped: the facts two to a row on a phone. They still stood 276px tall, "Tier 2 (document-verified)" broke over three lines in a 140px column, and the first answer only rose to 889px; the results page shares the strip, so it would have taken the same cramped columns.
- Kept: the facts themselves, unchanged and shared with the results page; the lede; "Answer the question" in the Answering box; the scheduled lede ("set out below"), which stays true.
- Still for later: the facts and the summary still say UTC; the page's text links still take the browser's outline; the answered count, reactions and follow sit as three blocks between the answers and the evidence; on the two-question poll the first answer starts at 686px, just under a 640px screen.

Fix:

- The facts and the poll's tags leave the header for their own section, "About this poll" (`pollFactsTitle`, in both languages), after "What it asks" and before the results, in every state, so the page reads as the mockup does: what it asks, then how it is run. A poll with no words shown (by ID, or hidden) goes straight from its header to the facts.
- A question card is numbered only on a poll of several questions, or one with a title of its own; the question that heads the page keeps its screen-reader heading ("Question 1") and loses the visible "1" and its column.
- Tests: `Worded` and `WordedDark` check that "About this poll" follows "What it asks" and holds the facts, and that no "1" numbers the card; `WordedQuestions` that both questions are still numbered. Both fail on the page before this iteration in both Storybook projects (4 failed). The poll summary Storybook projects (76 passed) and the website's tests (1,140 passed) pass.

Record: at 360 by 640 the first answer now starts at 589px, inside the first screen, where it started at 991px; on the two-question poll at 686px (1,068px); at 1280 by 800 at 528px (658px). "Answer the question" moves down by the facts' height and their heading, to 1,332px at 360 (1,256px) and 1,027px at 1280 (931px), which the answers above it now precede. The closed poll's page opens on its header and then "About this poll", as before but headed.

### The poll page: iteration 3

The stories of iterations 1 and 2 (`Components/Poll summary`), with `WordedClosing` (new: an open poll closing in three days, its answers sealed), the results' `Sealed` and the results page, at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, light and dark, read as a visitor in New York who taps a stream card closing in three days.

Review:

- **The page said the close in UTC under a card that said it on the visitor's clock.** The card read "Closes Jan 7, 7:00 PM EST" (the stream's iterations 22 and 23); the poll page it opened read "Closes January 8, 2026 at 12:00 AM UTC" in its facts, and the sealed results' sentence said the same, so the visitor met a different day for the same close and had to know their offset to reconcile them. The facts' opening read the same way.
- Kept: the facts' place after "What it asks" (iteration 2), the answers as links (iteration 1), the evidence's times in UTC, the board's own time ([[language]]), and the served page in UTC, which the browser redraws.
- Still for later: the page's text links still take the browser's outline; the answered count, reactions and follow sit as three blocks between the answers and the evidence; on the two-question poll the first answer starts at 686px, just under a 640px screen.

Fix:

- `PollFacts`, which the poll page and the results page share, draws Opens and Closes with `When local`, so "today" and "tomorrow" read as on a card.
- The sealed results' sentence takes its time from `useWhenWords` (`when.tsx`), the same clock and zone, always with the date, since it follows "on".
- Tests: `WordedClosing` checks the facts' "January 7 at 7:00 PM EST" and "December 31, 2025 at 7:00 PM EST", no UTC in the facts, the sealed sentence's "January 7, 2026 at 7:00 PM EST", and the evidence's "January 8, 2026 at 12:00 AM UTC"; `Sealed` expects New York's clock. Both fail on the page before this iteration in both Storybook projects (4 failed). The poll summary, results and results page stories now fix the visitor's zone and clock. Those stories (128 passed) and the website's tests (1,140 passed) pass.

Record: no layout moved. Before, the facts read "January 8, 2026 at 12:00 AM UTC" under a card's "Jan 7, 7:00 PM EST"; after, the card, the facts ("January 7 at 7:00 PM EST") and the sealed sentence name the same evening on the visitor's clock, and the evidence still says UTC.

### The poll page: iteration 4

The stories of iteration 3 (`Components/Poll summary`, `Poll results`, `Poll results page`, `Poll follow`, `Poll reactions`), read as a keyboard user tabbing down the page.

Review:

- **The page's text links drew the browser's focus.** Iteration 3 left this open. The answers, the evidence's toggle and the seal leading to the evidence draw the system's square 3px ring. The links drawn as underlined text (the short link, "Read the board, entry by entry", the moderation note's "See the moderation log", the results' "Check the count on the board" and "See the full results", the results page's four links, and the "Sign in" and "Verify your e-mail" links under follow and reactions) drew no focus style of their own, so they got the browser's thin rounded outline (`auto 1px`) across their underline. That made two looks for one thing on one page, the split the stream's iteration 33 closed on the card.
- Kept: the boxes' rings and the seal link's ring. They are drawn as boxes.
- Still for later: the answered count, reactions and follow still sit as three blocks between the answers and the evidence; on the two-question poll the first answer still starts at 686px, just under a 640px screen. Home's, a profile's and the account page's text links are their own surfaces' iterations.

Fix:

- Each of those links takes `focus-text` (`apps/web/src/styles.css`): a solid 3px outline in the ring's colour, 2px out. Follow's and reactions' links get it through their shared `LINK` classes.
- Tests: `focus-text.ts` is the stories' shared check (focused from the keyboard, `:focus-visible`, solid 3px, 2px out). `Poll summary` Default, Dark and Delisted, `Poll results` and `Poll results page` Default and Dark, and `Poll follow` and `Poll reactions` Unverified run it on each link. On the page before this iteration they failed in both Storybook projects (18 failed). Those stories (164 passed) and the website's tests (1,144 passed, the script budgets included) pass.

Record: no screenshots were taken. The stories check the computed focus style. An outline takes no layout space, so nothing moves when no link has focus.

The stories of iteration 4 (`Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedQuestions`, `Answered`, `Default` and `Dark`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, read as a visitor who has just tapped "Answer the question" or come back after answering, scrolling on to see what else the page offers.

Review:

- **Reactions and follow stood as two more full sections** between Answering and the evidence (iterations 1 to 4 left them for later). Each had its own heading, a lede, a control and a privacy note, stacked one under the other at every width: 600px of the 1280 by 800 screen (Reactions from 1,381px, the evidence from 2,001px), where Answering's box and the evidence each take one column of the same `max-w-3xl` width and the right half of the screen stood empty beside them.

A reaction isn't an answer and says nothing about whether you answered."; "Follow this poll to be told by e-mail when its results are in, whether or not you answer it. Following isn't answering and says nothing about whether you answered." The second sentence of each already says the first's "whether or not", and follow's lede opened by repeating its button. At 360 they ran to four and five lines.
- Kept: the two sections' order after Answering, their headings, the counts and controls, both privacy notes, and the promise that neither is an answer nor says whether you answered (T-10, T-11); neither shown on a hidden poll (T-68).

Fix:

- Reactions and follow share one row from 768px (`md`), each in its own column, top-aligned; on a phone they stack as before. With only one of them offered it takes the row alone.
- The ledes lose the repeated clause: "React to the poll itself. A reaction isn't an answer and says nothing about whether you answered." and "Get an e-mail when its results are in. Following isn't answering and says nothing about whether you answered.", with the Spanish catalog's to match.
- Tests: `Default` and `Dark` check both ledes and that the two sections share a top from 768px and stack below it, in both Storybook projects. On the page before this iteration they failed in both (4 failed). The poll summary, reactions and follow stories (114 passed) and the website's tests (1,146 passed) pass.

The card stands under a lede that opens "2 questions, answered together", with a "1" beside its question (and "Question 1" in its heading for screen readers), so "to question 1 of 2" said what both already say. In the 182px the number leaves the card at 360, it took the line to five lines, against three on a one-question poll; at 1280 to two lines, against one. The words are the stream card's, where they are needed: a card shows only question 1, unnumbered, and its top line leaves the count to them (the stream's iteration 6), so the card keeps them.
- Kept: the stream's, home's featured and a profile's cards saying "question 1 of N"; a rule a pick doesn't say leading the line (iteration 8); the number beside each question; question 2's answers as words under their rule.
- Still for later: the line is four lines at 360, in the column the number leaves; the embed's own "Pick an answer to question 1 of 2" is sharing's.

Fix:

- The poll page's line is the one-question sentence (`streamPick` at a count of 1), led by the rule where a pick doesn't say it, whatever the number of questions.
- Tests: `WordedQuestions` checks the line reads the one-question sentence with no "question 1 of"; `WordedQuestionsPhone` that it keeps to four lines at 360. Both fail on iteration 9's page in both Storybook projects (4 failed). The served page's test (`ssr.test.ts`) checks the poll of several questions; home's featured card keeps "question 1 of 2". The poll summary stories (82 passed), the home, profile and stream stories (112 passed) and the website's tests (1,169 passed) pass.

Record: on the two-question poll at 360 by 640 the line is four lines (five), the first answer starts at 554px (574px) and the page is 3,753px tall (3,773px); at 1280 by 800 one line (two), 461px (481px) and 2,588px (2,608px). One-question polls don't move.

### The poll page: iteration 11

The open, scheduled and closed poll's stories in its own words (`Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedDark`, `WordedQuestions`, `WordedQuestionsPhone`, `WordedScheduled` and `InShort`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, against the open poll's mockup (`everyday-poll-page.tsx`), read as a visitor who taps a friend's link.

Review:

- **A label in the evidence's type stood over the poll's own words.** Every poll page opened on "POLL 1011121314151617… · EVERYDAY POLL · ONE CHOICE" in small spaced capitals, two lines at 360 on a poll counted question by question, before the title. The mockup's hero opens on the byline and the question. The ID's prefix is evidence, which the everyday register keeps one step down ([[language]]) and the evidence shows in full with the definition's fingerprint; "Everyday poll" names the default to a visitor who has never met another kind; the counting is the facts' to say, and says it there ("Counting: One choice"). The label held the first answer 56px lower at 360 and 40px lower at 1280.
- Kept: the label on a poll shown by its ID (no words to show, or a hidden poll, T-68), where it is the page's only "Poll" before the ID that heads it; the label on the civic record, whose class is the claim the page goes on to qualify (T-14) and whose register is evidence-forward; the class in the evidence ("Kind"), the counting in the facts, the ID and fingerprint in the evidence (T-24, T-34).
- Still for later: the card's line above the answers is four lines at 360, in the column the number leaves (iteration 10).

Fix:

- An everyday poll shown in its own words has no label over its title; the title is the header's first line.
- Tests: `Worded` and `WordedDark` check the title is the header's first element and no "Poll 1011121314151617…" is drawn; `WordedQuestionsPhone` that question 1's second answer, not only its first, lies within the first 640px at 360. All three fail on iteration 10's page in both Storybook projects (6 failed). The served page's tests (`ssr.test.ts`) check the open poll has no label but carries its ID, and that its kind is still in the evidence; a poll shown by its ID and a civic one keep theirs. The poll summary stories (82 passed) and the website's tests (1,169 passed) pass.

Record: at 360 by 640 the first answer now starts at 457px (513px), and on the two-question poll at 534px (590px), its second answer ending at 638px, inside the first screen; the scheduled poll's first answer at 605px (661px). At 1280 by 800 at 376px (416px) and 421px (461px). Everything below the title moves up by those 56px and 40px; a poll by its ID, a hidden one and a civic one don't move.

### The poll page: iteration 12

The open and closed poll's stories (`Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedDark`, `WordedQuestionsPhone`, `Closed` and `Default`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, against the open poll's mockup (`everyday-poll-page.tsx`), read as a visitor who reached the foot of the facts and wants to answer or pass the poll on.

Review:

- **The panel's two actions stood one under the other at every width.** At 1280 the green "Answer the question" (232 by 50px) sat alone on its line, with "Share this poll" (256 by 56px), wider and taller, on the next line under it: two rows of buttons of different sizes in a 704px panel with room for both, reading as a ragged list rather than a pair of choices with one first. Reactions and follow, below, already go side by side from a tablet's width (iteration 5).
- Kept: the way in first and green, the share as the outline button beside it; on a phone, where both can't fit in the panel's 288px, the way in above the share, as before; the panel's lede, which says where answers are cast (T-10, T-11).
- Still for later: the line above the first question's answers is four lines at 360 on a poll of several questions (iteration 10); the panel's lede and "How do we know this is real?" both say each answer is sealed on the device that sends it.

Fix:

- The way in and the share are one wrapping row of one height: side by side wherever both fit, the way in first; wrapped to two lines on a phone. A closed poll's panel keeps the share alone.
- Tests: the open poll's stories (`Worded`, `WordedDark`) check, from a tablet's width, that the share sits beside the way in, at its top and of its height; both fail on iteration 11's page (2 failed). `WordedQuestionsPhone` checks that at 360 the share stays under the way in. The poll summary stories (82 passed) and the website's tests pass.

Record: at 1280 by 800 the share now sits at the way in's top, 12px to its right, both 56px high (it started 70px below the way in's top, 6px taller than it); the panel is 70px shorter, and reactions, follow, the evidence and reporting move up by that. At 360 nothing moves.

### The poll page: iteration 13

The open and closed poll's stories (`Components/Poll summary`: `Worded`, `WordedDark`, `Closed` and `Default`), at 360 by 640 and 1280 by 800, read as a visitor who reads from the Answering panel down into "How do we know this is real?".

Review:

- **The panel and the evidence said the same sentence.** Iteration 12 left it for later: on an open poll the panel's lede says each answer "is sealed on the voter's own device before anything is sent", and a screen further down the evidence opened on "Every answer is sealed on the device that sends it and listed on the public board". The visitor met the sealing twice, the second time as the evidence's first words, ahead of what the evidence is there to show: that the answers are public and can be counted again.
- **A closed poll said the recount twice.** Its panel read "This poll has closed. Its board stays public, so anyone can count it again.", and the evidence under it "… listed on the public board, which anyone can download and count again." Closed, the panel offers no way in, so the sealing was said only in the evidence and the recount in both.
- Kept: the panel's lede on an open or scheduled poll, beside the way in, where the open poll's mockup says the sealing (T-10, T-11); the evidence's launch line on who holds the key (T-14); the footer's claim, which is the frame's.
- Still for later: the line above the first question's answers is four lines at 360 on a poll of several questions (iteration 10).

Fix:

- While the poll takes answers or waits to open, the evidence's lede is "Every answer is listed on the public board, which anyone can download and count again."; the panel keeps the sealing.
- Closed, the panel says "This poll has closed and takes no more answers.", and the evidence "Every answer was sealed on the device that sent it and listed on the public board, which anyone can download and count again.", in the past, since no more will be. Both catalogs.
- Tests: `Worded`, `WordedDark` and `Closed` check the page says "sealed on" once and the recount once, in the panel while it is open and in the evidence alone once closed; all three fail on iteration 12's page in both Storybook projects (6 failed). The served page's tests (`ssr.test.ts`) check the closed poll's panel, its evidence lede and one "sealed on". The poll summary stories (82 passed) and the website's tests (1,169 passed) pass.

Record: on the open poll the evidence now opens on the board and the recount, a shorter lede; on the closed poll the panel is one short line under its heading and the evidence's lede carries the sealing. Nothing else moves.
